Others, because of their ferocity and voracity; of their barbarous cruelty, and uncivil demeanor, Esay 13.17,18. of their austerity and craft. Others, proceed further, to liken them to Bears, in the natural weakness of their heads; and in their rise and original, their rising being but sordid and base; as rude and unlikely, as a Bears Whelp is without form and shape. But we may not be too curious in these assimilations. They were less generous, more uncivil than the Chaldeans; though they were of great strength and durance, hardship and labor, and great Warriors, and much inclined to set upon Empires and States. Compared they are to silver, Chap. 2.32,39. as inferior to the former: to a Ram, Chap. 8.3,4.

*{it raised up itself on one side}* Or, *it raised up one dominion* Chald. *one Government*. *Madai* and *Paras*, two arms in one breast, now set up a joint government. The Trent Bible thus, *it stood in part.* With their Expositions raised hereupon: improper in themselves, and inconsistent with the true Text.

*{three ribs}* Meaning People, and their Relics, and Remains, devoured by the Persians, and their bear-like voracity. Which very thing the following words do intimate: for by three ribs are signified, *people of three coasts of the world*; from the East (which afore it held) it broke into the West, North, and South; as it is foretold, Chap. 8.4. Xenophon first Book of the Institution of Cyrus. Others translate *Rowes*, for *Ribs*. making three rows of teeth; and understanding thereby those three Presidents, Chap. 6.2. Or rather the three Kingdoms of Media, Persia, and Babylon, which devoured the other Kingdoms & Provinces, as was said; and made them their prey and meat, feeding upon their Weal, Customs, Revenues, and States.

*{they said}* Or, *it was said impersonally*: an Idiom in the sacred tongues not unusual. This is not the saying of *Haman* to *Ahasuerus* against the Jews, Esth. 3. But of the Princes to their Emperors generally; stirring up their spirits to further conquests to enlarge their Empires; as Darius the Mede, the Hyrcanians, Gobryas, and others had set on Cyrus to those first achievements; as Xenophon shows in his said book. Or else it is the speech of the Angels, ver. 10. Or chiefly of the ancient days, ver. 9. By whose means and secret instinct of his providence, the Persian Emperors were driven on to those great designs, and the enterprises and achievements thereof were made easy unto them, Esay 21.2.

*{Arise}* Stir up and prepare yourself; as Josh. 1.2. Gen. 13.17. Act. 1.27.

*{devour much flesh}* Cruelly destroy many *Nations* and People. A likely and suitable consequent, to the teeth of this devouring Beast.

**Verse 6.** *{another like a leopard}* The Empire of Alexander the Great, and Greece for the first partition. This rose after the former. A Leopard is a spotted beast, Jer. 13.23. yet fair, and sweet, and comely. He is swift, Hab. 1.8. watchful, cunning, and sudden, Jer. 5.6. Hos. 13.7. He is active, fierce, and strong; seeming outwardly harmless, yet crafty, cruel, and greedy of man's blood. And all alike was Alexander the Great; spotted with vices, yet graced also with singular virtues; his body, as Historians record, of a rare odoriferous smell: both body and mind answerable to these other qualifications. This is called a third Kingdom; the belly and thighs of brass; a Kingdom of brass, Dan. 2.32,39. The he-goat, Dan. 8.5. A mighty King, Dan. 11.3. They that would understand this Beast to be the Greek and Roman Empire joined together; to make way for the Turkish Empire to be meant by the 4. Beast, are in all very much mistaken, and need no refutation.

*{four wings of a fowl}* To show its wonderful swiftness. This, to the Leopard, is as much as to add much swiftness to swiftness, which indeed was admirable in Alexander the Great; in subduing Greece, destroying Thebes, overthrowing the Persians in the Adrastian fields, at the river Granicum, at Issus, a City of Cilicia: And lastly, and finally, at Arbela; So gaining the Empire of Persia, and Asia, and all in 6 years, flying as a Thunderbolt: besides his siege, and winning of Tyre; of Judea, and Syria, of Egypt, and many other Kingdoms, Nations, and People. He reigned in all but 12 years, six in his Monarchy. See, Chap. 8. ver. 5. touched not the ground.

*{four heads}* Either the four Tetrarchies in Alexander the great, his hereditary Kingdom of Macedon; which afterwards grew to be four Kingdoms; or, the four Kingdoms into which the Empire of Alexander was divided after his death; which his four chief Captains had, Dan. 8.8. & 11.4. 1 Mac. 1.9,10. *Seleucus Nicanor* had Asia the great; *Perdiccas*, and after him *Antigonus* had Asia the lesser; *Cassander*, and after *Philip* and *Lysymachus*, *Antipater* was King of *Macedonia*, and *Ptolomæus* had *Egypt*. For the other eight starting up upon *Alexander's* death, they rather coveted and sought kingdoms, than got and possessed them, and soon vanished and perished. The Beast, Apoc. 13.1. has 7 heads.

*{and dominion was given to it}* It was not of himself, nor of his own power; or, that *Alexander* did all those stupendous things, got all these Countries; for his army contained but 30000 men; and he overcame in one battle *Darius*, which had ten hundred thousand men; when instantly before the battle he was so heavy with sleep, that his eyes could scarce open, as the stories report; Therefore this power was given him of God, and so much himself confessed, when he met *Jaddus* the High Priest of the Jews, in his Pontifical attire, Joseph. Antiq. 6.11. Chap. 8. See Chap. 2.21,37,38. & 4.25. Prov. 8.15,16. Rom. 13.1,2. Joh. 19.11.

**Verse 7.** *{a fourth beast}* Not named, because it was the same Nation with the former; but is distinguished from the former by ten horns, whereof one most strange, having eyes, and mouth, and speaking very great words. Nor yet is it likened to any one certain kind of beast; by reason of the great varieties, and many diversities that were in it. And therefore this beast has somewhat the more obscurity and difficulty in it. And this, together with the many great things spoken of it, makes Daniel so particularly inquisitive after this fourth beast, ver. 19,20. yet by Dan. 8.9. we may find that the first meaning of this Prophecy has relation to the Kingdoms of Syria and Egypt, possessed by the Seleucidæ, and Lagidæ, the Images two legs, Dan. 2.33. The Roman Empire (though not here meant; and much less the Turkish Empire) did imitate this afterwards, in the persecution of the Church, the subversion and interdiction of God's service; and violence done to consciences: And the Revelation does many of these passages of Daniel to Antichrist. See Dan. 2.40. Yet the Seleucidæ that had the Kingdoms of Syria and Asia, seem to be most properly and punctually meant; by whom God's people the Jews suffered infinite evils and mischiefs. In the foretelling, and description whereof, the greatest part of the Chapters following, is taken up and employed.

*{dreadful and terrible}* More than the former Beasts; especially to God's people the Jews; to whom Daniel properly has respect; at Chap. 2.40. who never were more cruelly used, than by the Seleucidæ, Dan. 8.11,12. & 11.31,36.

*{great Iron teeth}* *Teeth*, that is, Leaders and Armies; and these great ones, not little teeth, but big and long grinders: and these of Iron, that is strong; as the word Iron is likewise used and applied to a furnace, a neck, a yoke, a rod, a wall. These teeth were instruments great to grind and diminish the Jews.

*{and stamped the residue with the feet of it}* Verse 23. That is, whom it did not consume, those it did most shamefully trample upon: troubling their minds, and bodies, with all kinds of cruelties; as, Psal. 8.13.

*{and it was divers}* As otherwise; so in regard his tyranny over the people of God tended to force them to idolatry; and to annihilate God's service, which none of the precedent Emperors had attempted.

*{and it had ten horns}* Not ten kinds of Government in the Roman Empire before Christ's coming, but personally ten Kings. And these not reigning altogether, a little before the end of the world, out of the ruins of the Roman Empire, as some weakly conceive; but succeeding one another in that kingdom of the Greeks, or Seleucidæ, until Antiochus comprehending him among the rest, ver. 24. For although he had successors, yet the people of God (in regard of whom these things are spoken) began after Antiochus to reassume their liberty, under the Asmoneans, or Maccabees, until Christ.

**Verse 8.** *{among them}* As 1 Sam. 17.34. not that his horn was the middlemost of the ten.

*{another little horn}* Or, *the last a little horn*, Verse 21,24. Chap. 8.9. This is not meant of Antichrist, as Papists would bear us in hand; to be the better thereby enabled to shift & shuffle off the true Antichrist from the Pope's Chair. But this is that Antiochus, called Epiphanes, or Epimanes rather; who is one and the last of the number of the ten Kings, which are signified here by ten horns. See Chap. 11.21.

*{before whom}* Chald. *before the face whereof*; as elsewhere before the face of the sword, of the bear, of the famine.

*{Three of the first horns plucked up by the roots}* That is, Kings plucked up by him by force and fraud. See ver. 20,24.

*{In this horn were eyes}* To signify his Natural sagacity, sharpness of wit, and craft, Dan. 8.23,25. & 11.23,32.

*{of man}* Here is noted the feigned humanity of Antiochus Epiphanes: who was not right heir, but as a private man, a younger brother, and allied in consanguinity to the kings, whom by much policy he defeated.

*{speaking great things}* He shall be exceeding haughty in words, 1 Macc. 1.25. and cruel in bloody Decrees; and a great blasphemer of God himself, persecuting the Saints, and commanding the abolishing of the Law, ver. 25. Dan. 8.23,25. & 36. 1 Mac. 1.46,47. See the like to this in Pagan Rome, Rev. 13.5.

**Verse 9.** *{I beheld}* And continued so.

*{till}* Not that he left off beholding after. And in like sense is the word used, 2 Sam. 6.23. Psal. 110.1. Matt. 28.20. & 1.25.

*{the Thrones were cast down}* Chald. *Thrones were set up*; or *speedily set*, or *they cast them*: speaking impersonally. See ver. 10,22,26. These are not the *Thrones* of the Beasts, wherein they ruled before. But of the *Ancient of days*. Thrones for Judgment, on which Judges should sit; one Throne for God, the other for the Son of David. Yet this is not meant of the last judgment, at the second coming of Christ, Rev. 20.4. Mat. 25.31,32. But of God's coming in judgment against *Antiochus Epiphanes*, in his time, and the remains of the former Empires. That this is the meaning, appears, by the words following; also by conference of verses, 8,11,20,22,25,26. that is, till God did enter into judgment against *Antiochus Epiphanes*, as we said; for the deliverance, and for the revenge of his people Jews. A figurative description it is; as Psal. 7.6,7. & 9.4,7. And when God comes to judgment, his enemies scatter, and their Thrones indeed are cast down, Esay 2.10, &c. Joel 2.11. Mal. 3.2. Psal. 9.4.

*{Ancient of days did sit}* A figurative representation of God, which was before all times; and of his glorious Majesty; set out unto us; as man's nature is able to apprehend some portion of his glory. And God, or rather the person of God the Father is here personally meant; as appears, ver. 13. For there Christ the Son of Man comes to him, is brought before him. As the like is, Apoc. 5.6,7. These and other visions of the Father, Son, and Holy Ghost, vouchsafed in Scripture, to Prophets, and Holy men of God, for special ends, by the special pleasure and dispensation of God's Will, are not warrants for us to make pictures and Images, of the persons of the Trinity by them, against the rule of the second Commandment.

*{did sit}* Psal. 9.4,7. as Judges do on the Tribunal. So showing his Judicial authority. Their not sitting in giving judgment, does in Countries invalidate their sentence.

*{garment white}* To shadow out the glory of his Majesty, and the Innocency and pure Integrity of his Justice, Psal. 104.1,2. & 51.4. Rom. 3.4. Thus Christ is set forth in garments white as snow, at his Transfiguration, Mar. 17.2. And again, Apoc. 1.14. The Angels, Mar. 28.3. Act. 1.10. The Saints in heaven, Apoc. 7.13. & Chap. 4.4. Joseph's vestures in Egypt were of fine white linen, Gen. 41.42.

*{the fiery flame}* *Our God is a consuming fire*, Heb. 12.29. Apoc. 1.14. See, Deut. 4.12.

*{and his wheels as burning fire}* God's Throne is here described with wheels, 1 Chron. 28.18. Ezek. 1.15. & 10.9. to show that God's providence moves everywhere, to govern all things; and provide for all things: and it points out God's celerity, and facility, in executing his judgments; as the lightning, &c. Mat. 24.27. See Apoc. 1.14,15.

**Verse 10.** *{a fiery stream issued}* Hereby is signified the inevitable and consuming force of God's judgments. See Psal. 50.2. & 97.2–7. Isa. 30.33. for the comfort of the godly, and the terror of the wicked.

*{thousand thousands}* Psalm 68.17. Revel. 5.11. 1 King. 22.19.

*{ministered unto him}* Psal. 103.20. & 34.7. Matth. 18.10. Heb. 1.14.

*{and ten thousand times ten thousand}* An infinite number of holy Angels, which were ready to execute his Commandment, Mat. 26.53. 2 King. 6.17. which number more largely explicates the same thing which was intended in the former number: as Mat. 18.21. Psal. 91.7. Not that from hence can be gathered a greater number of Angels that stand before God, than of those that minister unto him; or that any such different and distinction of Angels can be made out of this verse; as some Popish Schoolmen labor to do.

*{the Judgment was set}* The Judge, with his ministers and assistants, approving and praising his justice and judgments; as Psal. 119.137.

*{books were opened}* Terms taken from Judgments among men; In which the Inquests, proofs, confessions, and other evidences, and writings are produced to frame the Judgment by: and the Laws also are considered, whereupon the Judgment is made. See Rev. 20.12. where the like process is used in that last and general great Judgment day. Where the main Books are the Consciences of men, Rom. 2.14. and the Scriptures of God, Joh. 5.45. Both then opened by God in a special manner, Psal. 50.21. Here the impious actions of this horn. *Antiochus Epiphanes*, and evidences against him, are recited, as out of Books and Records, that for them, Judgment may pass upon him. It is not Antichrist, as we have eftsoon said, that is here judged and condemned, mentioned or meant.

**Verse 11.** *{because of the voice of the great words}* Chal. *from the voice*; that is, from the time it was heard. For from that time of *Antiochus Epiphanes*, and because of his great words against God, and the Church of God, then God began to shake and destroy the Kingdom of the Seleucidæ, with inward and with outward stirs.

*{even till the beast was slain}* As in the Images ruin he began with the Legs first, and not with the Head, which first perished: so here, first he speaks of the ruin of the last; for the safety of his own Nation: that the Chaldeans should pick no quarrel for this Vision. The beast then here is the fourth and last beast. The kingdom of the Seleucidæ, a while after *Antiochus Epiphanes* his death, 1 Mac. 6.8,13. was overthrown by the *Parthians*, and by *Tygranes* King of Armenia; as *Appianus Alexandrinus* shows.

*{and his body destroyed}* By *Pompey* the Great; taking *Tygranes*; abolishing the very show and seeming face of a Kingdom; and reducing Syria into a Province, as *Appian* and *Josephus* testify.

**Verse 12.** *{As concerning the rest of the beasts}* Chald. *the residue of the beasts*, or their *relics* and *remains*. They now perished with this; and were utterly taken away by the Romans. Or thus, speaking in the time past; as the three former monarchies have had an end, at the time that God appointed, although they flourished for a time; so shall this fourth have: and they that patiently abide God's appointment, shall enjoy the promises.

*{they had their dominion taken away}* Not at that very time; they had it before. The meaning is, that every great empire, and enemy, and persecutor of the Church, was destroyed before Christ's coming upon the earth, from time to time, each one in his time: though the people of them still continued and subsisted in some weak and low form of state. See Dan. 2.35. until all the remainders were brought under, by the Romans.

*{yet their lives were prolonged}* Chald. *a prolonging in life was given them:* had been given them: for a certain set time, by God's providence; which time they could no ways overpass. Thus whole kingdoms, though suffered long in sin, yet at last are totally ruined for sin.

**V. 13.** *{night visions}* Vers. 1.

*{behold}* Daniel's attentive heed; to the erection of the new and eternal kingdom of Christ.

*{one like the son of man}* Or, *as a son of man:* Which is meant of Christ, who had not yet taken upon him man's nature; neither was the son of David, according to the flesh, as he was afterward; but in vision only, and apparition here, Daniel sees him coming from the Father, and returning to his Father again. The Jews grant Christ to be here so termed. And in the four Evangelists, our Savior terming himself *Son of Man*, most graciously calls us to weigh this text. This phrase, *like the Son of Man*, is used also of him, in vision, after his ascension, Rev. 1.13. & 14.14. and may intimate somewhat more in him, than mere man. See Job. 1.14. Those places, Rom. 8.3. Phil. 2.7. show the state and condition of his human nature.

*{came}* To work the mystery of our redemption upon earth.

*{with the clouds of heaven}* Rose not out of the sea; as those former beasts and empires did. But came in the clouds of heaven, from heaven, Psal. 104.3. Act. 1.9,11. Mat. 24.30. & 26.64. Rev. 1.7. & 14.14,15,16.

*{to the ancient of days}* Christ, after his resurrection, ascended into heaven; and is set at the right hand of his Father: and from him has obtained all power in heaven and in earth. See Ezek. 1.26. Joh. 16.16,28. Act. 1.9. & 2.34,36. Heb. 1.3,4. Rev. 1.13. & 14.14.

*{and they brought him near before him}* *was brought*; impersonally: Or, the holy angels did it. For though Christ ascended by virtue of his divinity; yet angels are his ministering spirits, to attend on him, Heb. 1.6,14. This is not meant of his second coming to judgment; nor can be so properly applied thereunto.

**V. 14.** *{dominion and glory}* This is meant of the beginning of Christ's kingdom, Mat. 28.18. Joh. 3.35. When God the Father, at his ascension, specially, gave unto him all dominion as to the mediator; to the intent that he should govern, here, his Church on earth continually, till the time that he brought them to eternal life. This is the sitting at the right hand of his Father, Psal. 2.4–10. And this power he exercises in the midst of his foes: maintaining his Church, and overthrowing his enemies; as it is, Psal. 110.1,2.

*{and a kingdom}* The conclusion of our Lord's prayer, Mat. 6.13. calls into our minds this speech; and teaches of the *King of Eternity*, 1 Tim. 1.17. who joins Jews and Gentiles, in one kingdom.

*{that all people}* Psal. 2.8. Ephes. 1.20,21,22,23. Phil. 2.9,10,11. Rev. 19.16. Jews and Gentiles. Not so was it with any earthly monarch whatsoever.

*{his dominion}* Chald. *saltan:* Whence possibly the word *sultane,* or *soldan,* might arise.

*{an everlasting dominion}* Chap. 2.44. Psal. 145.13. Mic. 4.7. Luk. 1.33. To endure after his ascension forever. This is the dominion, and power of his office, given to the person of the mediator, in regard of both his natures; and distinct from the essential power of the divine nature. And this argues not the constant visibility, and outward flourishing estate of any one particular Christian Church on earth: but the duration of Christ's dominion in his Church, in this world, even in the midst of his enemies, Mat. 16.18. & 28.18,20.2. Psa. 110.2. and the complement thereof in the Church triumphant forever.

**V. 15.** *{I Daniel}* The interpretation of the whole vision, by an angel, at the request of Daniel.

*{was grieved in my spirit}* Much perplexed and wounded, as if a weapon had pierced through him.

*{body}* Chald. *sheath;* as if his spirit had left it: and his body were become as a dead sheath.

*{troubled me}* According to the manner of prophets, Daniel specially; who, after their ecstasies, through the greatness and brightness of the sight of God's majesty, which did dazzle their senses, tire their spirits, and overdo their natural faculties, were astonished and fainted. See Chap. 7.28. & 8.27. and 10.8,16. and troubled also at the wonder of the vision, and in that he knew not what should be meant by it. And thus was Pharaoh troubled at his divine dream, Gen. 41.8. and Nebuchadnezzar, Dan. 2.1. which stirred them up so much to know the interpretation of them.

**V. 16.** *{unto one of them that stood by}* Namely, one of the angels that appeared in this vision; as vers. 10.

*{the truth of all this}* The proper meaning.

**V. 17.** *{these great beasts}* Likely so called, in consideration of their beastly and savage dealing, against the families of our Lord's ancestors, the house of Zorobabel, and the nation that should have their kings from it.

*{four kings}* Kingdoms, vers. 23,24. whereof, Chap. 2.39,40. He may seem to use the term kings, rather than kingdoms, that the next verse, short in the Chaldee, should not be mistaken; and that none should think of *four kings, to hold a kingdom forever and ever*, as the translation of that verse will bear. The prophet seems not unwilling to hide his mind from savage heathen.

*{shall arise}* The Chaldean kingdom was risen already, and in present being. He speaks, as respecting the greater part.

*{arise}* Arising here, may, perhaps have a kind of relation to their rising over the kingdom of the saints, and withholding it.

*{out of the earth}* Sea, vers. 3. They are distinct, Revel. 13.1,11. Here one is an explication of the other; meaning the world, and the tempestuous troubles, and rolling changes of it.

**V. 18.** *{But}* Or the verse may be rendered thus; *And they shall take the kingdom of the saints of the most high; but they that are saints, shall possess the kingdom forever, even forever & ever.* Meaning, though those four kingdoms, which Daniel speaks of, should, for a time, rule over the Jews; (who are called the saints of the highest here, and after, vers. 21,22.) yet Christ coming should set up a kingdom, to rule forever. But our own translation, best agrees with the Chaldee distinction, here used; and with the context following, in this verse, and vers. 22, & 27.

*{the Saints}* The house of Zorobabel, by Abiud, and Rhesa, Mat. 1.13. Luk. 3.27. (though they be not mentioned among Zorobabel's sons, 1 Chron. 3.19.) and the godly, of their nation; to wit, the Church of God, in Christ her head, first, and afterward the whole body, which shall be perfectly united with him, shall receive from God, a spiritual and everlasting kingdom. See vers. 22. Isa. 60.12. Rev. 1.6. & 5.10. whereof the Maccabees' principality was but a shadow; and was between the reign of Antiochus, and the coming of Christ.

*{of the most high}* Chald. *high ones;* that is, *things,* or, *places:* that is, of the most high things, because God has chosen them out of this world, that they should look up to the heavens, whereon all their hope depends: or highest, in the plural number, to teach us of the divine persons, as Abraham speaks plurally, Gen. 20.13. and David, 2 Sam. 7.22. Here, it was fit in a distinct vision of the son, and of the father; as thrones also are plural, ver. 9. See Eccles. 5.8.

*{shall take the kingdom}* That kingdom, ver. 14. not that the beasts should take the kingdom of the saints of the most High, or of the most high God, and possess it forever, as the Trent Bible renders it, and forces his followers commonly to expound it. But the saints should take and possess it; as has been said.

**V. 19.** *{Then I would}* Not satisfied with that short answer, he desires to know more.

*{of the fourth beast}* Of this specially: for of this, most and last was mentioned in the vision, ver. 7,8, &c. many strange and prodigious things spoken of it: and therefore Daniel repeats them here again, with some enlargements.

*{was diverse}* Vers. 7. It was partly unlike, because two kings, Syria and Egypt, both claimed right over Judah, since the days of Seleucus Nicanor, and Ptolomy Lagi, and made continual wars for it: but diverse also in cruel spoiling the Jews, and in hatred of their religion: which Babel, Madai, and Alexander, more favored. See ver. 23.

*{from all the others}* Chald. *from all those.*

*{and his nails of brass}* Or, *steel.* This not mentioned in the vision, ver. 7. This signifies, that this kingdom of the Seleucidæ, should tear in pieces the Jews, as a wild beast tears his prey with his claws. This we see in the history of Josephus, and the Maccabees.

**V. 20.** *{ten horns}* Of the kings Seleucidæ and Lagidæ, which greatly troubled the kingdom, when the house of Zorobabel should have reigned to the glory and comfort of all the world, vers. 8.

*{and of the other}* Particularly of that, because the things spoken of it, did seem strange and prodigious, and prognosticate and portend some great and fearful, direful, and ominous matters; and therefore he does here again recount them, as vers. 8.11.

*{before whom three fell}* See Annotations on vers. 8. and vers. 24.

*{whose look}* This not mentioned in the vision, vers. 7,8. there called a little horn; for such was Antiochus Epiphanes at the beginning. Here his looks more stout than his fellows; for so in the end he exceeded them all in power, and great enterprises, Chap. 8.10. & 11.37.

**V. 21.** *{made war}* See the like, Apoc. 11.7. & 13.7. Thus the wicked, even the Babylonians in their time, Zach. 1.19,21. Antiochus in his time; and Antichrist in his time likewise, prevail over the saints, vers. 19. of this chapter. This made Daniel so inquisitive after this beast, and the particularities of it: Though this war with the saints, and prevailing over them, was not specified in the vision before.

*{with the Saints}* With the Jews, as the books of the Maccabees do show. Papists, understanding this little horn of their conceited Antichrist, that should reign but three years and a half before the end of the world, and Christ's second coming to judgment, do make the saints here, to be the elect and godly, living in that short time. And this war to be that of Antichrist against them: and hereunto they apply, Matth. 24.21,24. and Revel. 13.7.

**V. 22.** *{Ancient of days came}* See vers. 9. Till God showed his power in the person of Christ; and by the preaching of the Gospel, gave unto his Son rest.

*{and judgment was given}* Neither was this mentioned before. The meaning is, till the Ancient of days came, vers. 9. and gave the power of judgment to them, in Christ the head; in whom, the saints are the judges of the world, 1 Cor. 6.2,3. Mat. 19.28. and they obtained, by the preaching of the Gospel, a famous name in the world, and became, in name and truth, the kingdom of God.

*{to the saints}* To the people of God, ver. 18.21,25. Chap. 8.24. & 11.30. To them, and on their behalf; to possess the kingdom.

*{possessed the kingdom}* Vers. 14. It was given to Christ the saints here possessed it, in him their head; they possess it as partners with him, and partakers in their degree and capacity. This must arm God's people with patience, under the sorest persecutions, and fill them with comfort and expectation in the meantime.

**V. 23.** *{Thus he said}* To wit, the angel, of whom he asked information concerning this matter, ver. 16. answering in order to the four points of Daniel's question.

*{the fourth beast}* The kingdom of Asia; or Syria, or of the Seleucidæ; so called, because the far greater part of these kings, were Seleucidæ, two only of the Lagidæ excepted: we may not with the Romish Church, understand this of the Roman Empire, as has been said.

*{which shall be diverse}* In cruelty; and is showed in the next words, Not *greater* than all kingdoms; as the Trent Bible has it: to make it thereby to be understood of the Roman Empire.

*{devour the whole earth}* Not all the world: but the holy land, namely Judea; as appears by vers. 21,25. breaking and trampling it more than the three former beasts did. See Luk. 23.44.

**V. 24.** *{And the ten horns}* Verse 7,8.

*{are ten Kings}* viz. *Seleucus Nicanor, Antiochus Soter, Antiochus Theos, Seleucus Callinicus, Ptolomeus Evergetes Lagida, Seleucus Ceraunus, Antiochus Magnus, Ptolomeus Philopater Lagida, Seleucus Philopater, Antiochus Epiphanes.* More kings reigned after these ten: but they possessed not Judea; and so could not afflict the Jews; God's people: and therefore in this vision, which is vouchsafed for the Jews' sake, no notice is taken of them. Of these things we read in Appianus, Alexandrinus, Memnon, Josephus and others. The Papists here, constant and consonant to their former error, do make these ten horns, answerable to the ten toes of the feet of Nebuchadnezzar's image, Chap. 2.33,41,42. and understand both of the Roman Empire, to be divided into ten kings or kingdoms a little before the end of the world: and to the same purpose they apply the ten horns of that *seven-headed beast,* Rev. 13.1. & 17.3,7,12. And yet they miserably vary among themselves about these kings, and their number. But, as we have eftsoons said, Daniel's prophecy and visions meddle not at all with the Roman Empire; but as it may in some respects relate to the first coming of Christ.

*{and another shall arise after them}* *Antiochus Epiphanes,* the last of those ten; and the last of the Seleucidæ, that reigned over Judea: for in his time the Maccabees delivered the Jews from his tyranny and persecution. Most Papists apply this to Antichrist; some, to Mahomet.

*{diverse from the first}* More cruel to the Jews, and perfidious, than the former nine kings, 1 Mac. 1.11.

*{three Kings}* Ptolomæus Philopater, whom he, joining forces with his father, and brother, drove out of Syria. Antiochus the Great, (his father) and Seleucus Philopater, his elder brother, whom he poisoned by Heliodorus. Others, more rightly, instead of his father Antiochus Magnus, do put for the third, Demetrius the son of his brother Seleucus, the right heir. In applying this to Antichrist, Papists rather say nothing, or confess they can only divine.

**V. 25.** *{and he shall speak}* Chap. 8.24,25. & 11.28,30. 1 Mac. 1.46. See the like, Rev. 13.5. 2 Thes. 2.4.

*{against the most high}* That is, speak blasphemies against God; and make wicked decrees and proclamations against God's Word, and send through all his dominion, tyrannically and cruelly to destroy all the Jews and godly people that did profess and practice it. This is largely set down, Chap. 11.

*{Saints}* Ver. 21. Chap. 8.24,25.

*{to change times}* Sabbaths, Passover, Pentecost, expiation-day, the Feast of Tabernacles, new moons, the seven years' rest, and such like, Dan. 8.11. and 11.31. 1 Mac. 1.47,48. miserable shifts are made by Papists to apply this to their conceited Antichrist.

*{and laws}* All Moses' ceremonies, and laws, 1 Mac. 1. taking upon him a divine power, usurping God's own authority over his people; changing and abolishing God's laws, making new laws of his own clean contrary to God's; and forcing most cruelly the people's obedience unto them.

*{shall be given}* He shall give such laws; and the saints shall be given into his hand, to be oppressed, worn out, and overpowered by him.

*{time and times}* A *time* here is a year: *times,* two years: and a dividing, or particle of time, seems to be ten days; as the history of the Maccabees sets it down, from the year 145 month 9 day 15 of the reign of the Greeks or Seleucidæ, until the year 148 month 9 day 25. 1 Macc. 1.54. and Chap. 4.52. See Chap. 12.7. and the like, Chap. 14.16. and Revel. 12.14. and Chap. 13.5.

*{and the dividing of time}* Trent Bible thus; *half a time.* And so they expound it, half a year: contrary to the warrant of the text, and the truth of the story.

**V. 26.** *{But the judgment shall sit}* Ver. 9. God, *the ancient of days,* the just judge shall sit in judgment; and thenceforth not cease to execute his judgments, to take away, consume, and utterly destroy that little horn, and the whole kingdom of the Seleucidæ; the fourth beast, vers. 9.11. And this was done by the Maccabees, the Parthians, Tygranes; and lastly, and utterly by the Roman Pompey the Great; as Appianus in his book of the Wars of Syria, and other authors do record. And these destructions are handled in Ezek. Chap. 38. & 39. And about 150 years afore our Lord's birth, are spent in this wasting of Syria and Egypt, the Seleucidæ and Lagidæ, by wars among themselves, and with the Maccabees and others; as has been said.

**V. 27.** *{And the Kingdom}* Vers. 14. Luk. 1.33. He shows wherefore the beast should be destroyed; to wit, that his Church might have rest and quietness, & a kingdom. Which rest, although they do not wholly enjoy here, yet they have it in hope; and by the preaching of the Gospel, enjoy the beginning thereof; which is meant by these words, *under the heavens:* and therefore he here speaks of the beginning of Christ's kingdom in this world, the kingdom of the Messias; when Christ begins to reign in the hearts of the faithful amongst all the nations of the whole world, Mar. 16.15.

*{shall be given to the people}* Ver. 14. It was given to the Son. Here to the people of the most High. The faithful have it by the participation that they have with Christ their head, 1 Cor. 4.9. The head communicating its glory to its body. For Christ with all he has as redeemer, is considered as ours; His righteousness, sufferings, kingdom, ours, Rom. 8.32. Hence are those speeches, 1 Pet. 2.9. Rev. 1.6. & 5.10. Mat. 11.12.

*{whose Kingdom}* Upon this all the New Testament goes, and Paul to Timothy speaks, 1 Tim. 1.17. He advances the Gospel, and the praise of the *King eternal, uncorrupt, invisible, God only wise*. Also the Revelation after the destruction of Jerusalem, is a heavenly commentary upon this part.

*{is an everlasting Kingdom}* Vers. 14. Heb. 12.26,27. though after a thousand years *Satan was loosed for a little season*, Rev. 20.23. Mat. 16.18.

*{and all Dominions}* Or, *Rulers;* that is, some of every sort that bear rule; some of them, in all parts of the world. So 1 Tim. 2.4.

**V. 28.** *{Hitherto is the end of the matter}* Here end the visions of Daniel; which the prophet penned in the tongue known over the East, and South; wherein the Jews are not described plainly to be the people, for whom God plagues the kingdoms: and that the heathen might be drawn to think somewhat better of the God of heaven. And more properly here ends the angel's interpretation, and what he revealed touching the future state of the Church.

*{As for me Daniel}* Chal. *I Daniel.*

*{my cogitations}* Vers. 15. Chap. 8.27. & 10.8. Though he had many motions in his heart which moved him to and fro to seek out this matter cunningly: yet he was content with that which God revealed, and kept it in memory, and wrote it for the use of the Church, 1 Cor. 4.6.

*{my Countenance changed}* Chald. *my brightnesses changed;* that is, the brightness of my countenance. I became all pale and wan; both in regard of the raptures of the spirit; as also through horror of the predictions against God's people, ver. 23,25.

*{But I kept the matter in my heart}* Faithfully to deliver it to the Church of God. Thus was Daniel affected in his heart with God's Word, and the revelation of his will; and so should we be.

## CHAPTER 8

**V. 1.** *{In}* This chapter expounds the second, and the seventh chapters. For here is a third description of those kingdoms or monarchies; and that more largely, and clearly, than formerly. Only the Chaldean monarchy is omitted here; and so in the visions and chapters following, as being now at the point of expiration; and in the time of the visions and prophecies following clean extinct. And therefore also this chapter, and the rest that follow, are not written by Daniel in the Chaldee tongue; as all the former were, which concerned the Chaldeans, and behoved them to know. But in the Hebrew tongue, for the peculiar use of the Church of the Jews; to inform them of their estate in future times, to arm them with patience, against persecutions coming; and comfort them in expectation of joyful deliverance at the last. And these visions are commanded to be shut up and sealed, Chap. 8.26. & Chap. 12.4. as being for future ages; and lest the naming of those heathen persecutors (as is done in these visions) being known to them, might create further danger to God's people.

*{the third year}* and therefore before that in the fifth chapter.

*{unto me, even unto me}* Vers. 15. to distinguish these visions, from those of Nebuchadnezzar, Chap. 2.4. Daniel's person adds to the authority of these visions, 2 Tim. 3.14.

*{at the first}* In the first year of Belshazzar, Chap. 7.1.

**V. 2.** *{in a vision}* Not in a dream, but in vision: so twice in this verse, and once in the former. See Annotations on Chap. 2.19. & on Chap. 7.1. And yet this might be in the night on his bed, in his sleep.

*{at Shushan}* In vision and ecstasy there; so much the former words import, and no more. And therefore then he was most likely at Babylon in body; This being but the third year of Belshazzar's reign: and so vers. 27. So Ezekiel being bodily present in Mesopotamia, in the visions of God was brought into the land of Israel, Ezek. 8.3. & 40.2. men in visions, as in dreams, many times think themselves to be corporally in places, where indeed they are not. The arms and breast, and the bear, are here now again in the third vision, and more clearly described than formerly; for the most certainty of the thing; as Gen. 41.25,32. and that Judah should be the more skillful in this matter. Shushan, or Susis, whence the country about it is called *Susiane.* Of this *Shushan,* we read, Neh. 1.1. Esth. 1.2,5. & 2.3. & 3.15. & 8.14. & 9.6. The word *Shushan,* signifies *a lily*, in the Greek; *a fountain, or well*, in that country language.

*{Palace}* The place of royal residence, the metropolitan city of Persia; where was a most strong tower or castle, most strongly fortified for the keeping of the Persian treasure. Brought to this perfection afterwards by Darius the son of Hystaspes, as Pliny relates. Here the kings of Persia afterwards kept their court, and made their stay one part of the year, Esth. 1.2,5. Otherwhiles they did, at Persepolis, and Ecbatana, *Q. Curtius,* lib. 5. The word also signifies a castle, court, and temple.

*{Province}* Some translations read *Region;* some, *City.*

*{of Elam}* Gen. 10.22. the west part of Persia; next to the confines of Babylon. See Act. 2.9.

*{river of Ulai}* Trent Bible, *Port or gate of Ulai:* without any warrant, and against the Hebrew text.

*{Ulai}* Or, *Eulæus;* that compassed the city Shushan round about. This river was held by them in some sacred esteem. And therefore their kings drunk of this river only, and had the water of it carried along to remote places with them. See Plin. l. 6. c. 27. It seems also to be called Choaspes; as usually the same river, specially at diverse places, is commonly called by diverse names; as likewise the famous rivers, Danubius, Tygris, and Euphrates are. Here Daniel at the river Ulai. And again, Hiddekel, Chap. 10.4. as Ezekiel at the river Cheber, Chap. 1.1. and John at Jordan, Mat. 3.16,17.

**V. 3.** *{My eyes}* The eyes of his mind, in his intellectual, spiritual visions, illuminated with divine light; and the eyes of his body, having those similitudes of corporal things formed in the inward sense of his imagination.

*{before the river}* Ulai, at Susis, where was the ordinary residence and court of the kings of Persia, as was said: and therefore very fit that this vision was seen there.

*{a Ram}* A figure of the Persian and Median Empire, signified by the two horns: as vers. 20. by the image's breast, and arms of silver, Dan. 2.32. and by the bear, Dan. 7.5. We may be here too curious in searching out reasons of this assimilation, between the ram and this monarchy. But sure we are, that this monarchy was more useful, favorable, and beneficial to the Jews, than any of the other.

*{two horns}* The word is of the dual number. These are Medes and Persians.

*{high}* Not crooked, as rams' horns use to be. In the horns, and head; the force of rams does consist.

*{one was higher}* The kingdom of Persia, which though founded after that of Media, grew far greater than it, by the victories of Cyrus the Persian, who gained Media, Babylon, and many countries more.

*{than the other}* Heb. *the second.*

*{last}* The kingdom of Persia, was later than that of Media. Cyrus the Persian succeeded Darius the Mede; and Persians still succeeded Cyrus in that empire.

**V. 4.** *{Westward}* Heb. *the Sea,* that is, the midland sea, which is westward from Shushan, and Persia, which were in the east part of the world (see Annotations on Ezek. 10.3.) He understands hereby Babylon, Syria, Cappadocia, lesser Asia and Græcia.

*{and Northward}* Lydia, Armenia, Scythia, Pontus, the Caspians, and northern nations.

*{and Southward}* Arabia, Ethiopia, Egypt, Lybia, &c. See Chap. 7.5. Esth. 1.1.

*{no beasts might stand before him}* That is, no kings or nations, could hinder his enterprises and actions, Jer. 51.30. though the Assyrians, Crœsus, and many other kingdoms and nations assisted the Babylonians. Thus God by the Persian, revenged the cause of his Church and people against the Babylonians, Jer. 51.56,50,15,18,24,25,28,29,34.

*{out of his hand}* This improperly ascribed to a ram. The meaning is plain and usual; out of his *power.*

**V. 5.** *{An he-goat}* By this is figured the kingdom of Greece and Macedonia, under Alexander, vers. 21. All the brass and iron, Chap. 2.33,39. also the leopard, and the fourth beast, Dan. 7.6,7. are in this he-goat, or buck. And this is a sweet commentary upon them. This prophecy was showed by Jaddua the high priest, to Alexander, when he entered into Jerusalem, some two years before he conquered Darius Codomannus, the last Persian emperor, *Josephus, l. 11. c. 8. Antiquit. Jud.*

*{from the west}* From Greece and Macedon, westward from Asia; where the prophet was, and where the Persians reigned.

*{whole earth}* So Dan. 2.39. all Asia; all that land wherein Daniel was; that which was most esteemed, and best known in all the world. Alexander complained, that he had not another world to overcome. *unus pellæo juveni non sufficit Orbis;* Juvenal, Satyr. 10.

*{and touched not the ground}* Or, *none touched him in the earth*: so swiftly great Alexander conquered the East in 6 years; as flying above the earth, rather than going on the earth; an hyperbole, as that also is, John 21.25. See Chap. 7.6. *four wings of a fowl*, added to a swift leopard. Thus Virgil speaks of Camilla: and Ovid of Atalantæ.

*{a notable horn}* Heb. *a horn of sight*. Alexander was the notablest for his strange success in wars, of any that ever warred, ver. 8.21. It is called *the great horn.*

*{between his eyes}* As an unicorn, whose strength is mentioned, Numb. 23.26. Job 39.12–15. And by the eyes, may be figured as Alexander's own sharpness of wit, quick in sight and foresight, and clearness of wisdom; so also his wise captains, that were as eyes to him. Such were Parmenio, and others. This *horn* not seen in a goat; as neither the *four Horns,* ver. 8.

**V. 6.** *{Came to the Ram}* A description of Alexander's acts against Persia, whereby he overthrew that kingdom, and made himself lord of Asia.

*{In the fury of his power}* This place is a sweet abridgment of all great Alexander's conquests.

**V. 7.** *{moved with Choler}* At Granicon water in his first fought field he did well.

*{and smote the ram}* At Issicon, a town in Cilicia, the second battle, he did better: and took prisoners Darius his mother, wife, and children among others.

*{and brake his two horns}* Thirdly, at Gangamela, he killed of men about 600000. as some write, and got the empire.

**V. 8.** *{he-goat}* The word signifies a *young* he-goat: as Alexander was young, at that time about 26. years old; and about 20 when he began to set upon his great wars. See Zach. 14.3.

*{the great horn}* Alexander died suddenly in the flower of his age, and in the middle of his victories, vers. 22. when he was 33 years old, and had held the monarchy six or seven years; as *Arianus, Plutarchus,* and *Diodorus Siculus* do report. Such is the instability of earthly monarchs, and all worldly glory; even when it is at the height, Psal. 82.6,7.

*{and for it came up}* Or, *stood up for it:* after diverse horrible slaughters and murders in *Alexander's* family; and conflicts and bloodshed among his chieftains and commanders, a while foregoing, Chap. 7.8,11,21.

*{four notable ones}* Out of the nation; but not in his power, ver. 22. Seleucus Nicator had the kingdom of the East, Syria, and Babylon, and Persia. Perdiccas, Cassander, and Philippus Aridæus had Macedonia, in the West. Antigonus had Asia the less, and more, northward: and Ptolemy Lagides, had Egypt southward: and at last these were reduced to two, the Seleucidæ, and Lagidæ.

**V. 9.** *{and out of one of them}* That is, out of Seleucidæ that reigned in Babylon and Syria.

*{came forth}* Chap. 7.8. and 11.21.

*{a little horn}* Chap. 7.8. Antiochus Epiphanes, who was of a servile and flattering nature; and also there were other between him and the kingdom: and therefore is here called *the little horn,* because neither princely conditions, nor yet any other thing was in him, why he should obtain the kingdom, Chap. 11.21.

*{toward the South}* That is, toward Egypt, which Antiochus seized upon, Chap. 11.25,40,43. Himself had, and held the North; that is, Syria, and Babylon.

*{the East}* That is Persia, which was also conquered by Antiochus.

*{pleasant land}* So is Judea, or the land of Israel called in Scripture; In regard of its great prerogative, temporal and spiritual; it being *the glory of all lands*; the ornament, pleasure, and nobleness: or as it were the pleasant roe, Prov. 5.19. Thus again, Dan. 11.16,41,45. Zech. 7.14. Of this land, see Ezek. 20.6,15. yet it most was for Jerusalem's sake, Psal. 48.2. and God's Temple, and service there. See these enterprises of Antiochus upon Judea, chap. 11.30. and following verses.

**Verse 10.** *{to the host}* Or, *against the host,* ver. 24. Chap. 7.18. and 11.32. Antiochus raged against the Church of God, which is a heaven where God dwells in grace; and where the faithful do hold the place of Angels. See Esay 14.13. He made way against religion, and the Temple, and the holy Jews.

*{Cast down}* That is, slew some of the choicest, in Church and commonwealth; in offices and graces: some of the elect, which are called the host of heaven, and stars; because they are separated from the world, and whose names are written in heaven, Chap. 12.3. Luk. 10.20. Thus he brought heaven under his feet, to trample on it. Thus he dealt with old Eleazarus, and with the mother, and her seven sons, 2 Macc. 6,7. Chapters.

**Verse 11.** *{Even to the prince of the host}* Ver. 25. Or *against;* daring to push, not only at the high Priest, or Judas Maccabeus, but at God himself; The Son of God, who as a Prince in a singular manner reigns in and over his people. And this Antiochus did, by bearing an hostile spirit against the God of the Jews; by blaspheming him, and violating his Temple, service, and glory; as is shown in the words following. See Chap. 7.25. and 11.22,36. 2 Macc. 5.11.——17. and vers. 21,24,26.

*{by him}* Or, *from him: by him,* that is, by Antiochus, rather than from him; that is, from the Prince of the host.

*{daily sacrifice}* Ver. 13. Namely, the morning and evening sacrifice; the law whereof is in Exod. 29.38, &c. Numb. 28.3. and also all other sacrifices, and feasts, and the whole worship of God; which Antiochus to his utmost power by all means did labor to put down, as the books of Maccabees do show, 1 Macc. 1.44.——64.

*{place of his Sanctuary}* The Temple of Jerusalem, called by many several names in Scripture. As the Church is called by many names and titles in the Old and New Testament.

**Verse 12.** *{an host was given him against the daily sacrifice}* Or, *the host was given over, for the transgression against the daily sacrifice.* The host of the Jews was given over to him and his power, because of their transgression against the daily sacrifice. Or, the faithful shall be in great danger of apostatizing from the true religion: and Antiochus shall give them many occasions to do it, and shall seek to induce them to it by force, craft, and all means: and the Lord shall put them to this trial, Chap. 11.32,35. 1 Mac. 1.

*{by reason of transgression}* Transgression the reason and cause of all calamities.

*{cast down the truth}* All use, preaching, and profession of God's truth, and doctrine of salvation, shall be forbidden and suppressed by Antiochus; who did seek to destroy the very books of the Law, 1 Mac. 1.59. See *Annotations* on vers. 26. See Esay 59.14.

*{and it practised and prospered}* That is, that *Horn:* and *Horn* being of the feminine gender, has these words likewise of the feminine gender to agree with it: and vers. 24. these words are put in the masculine gender, to agree with the word *King,* of the masculine gender in ver. 23. Thus the most wicked men do prosper, even in their vilest practices against God's people, by reason of their transgressions. So did Nebuchadnezzar in his time.

**Verse 13.** *{one Saint speaking}* That is, a certain Saint: meaning, that he heard by an inward hearing, one of the Angels asking this question of Christ; though some say, of another, and higher Angel. Angels desire to see into the mysteries of God, 1 Pet. 1.12. Eph. 3.10. and to know a speedy end of so great a devastation of God's Saints and service, as was here foreshowed.

*{and another Saint}* Heb. *and a certain Saint;* that is, either the former, or another holy Angel. See Zach. 2.3.

*{unto that certain Saint}* Heb. *unto a certain one, or a secret one, or a marvellous one, or the Numberer of secrets;* or, *the wonderful Numberer.* The Hebrew word is *Palmoni;* which may seem to be compounded of two words, *peloni,* and *almoni:* and these are used by the Hebrews, when they will note out a certain man, and yet not name him; as Ruth 4.1. 1 Sam. 21.2. and 2 King. 6.8. Mat. 26.18. The Greeks express this by *ὁ δεῖνα.* See Ezra 4.10. This Saint unnamed, or that wonderful Numberer; composing the word of *pala,* and *manah,* is most commonly and probably conceived to be Christ, the Wisdom, and the Word of the Father; who knows all the secrets of his wisdom most perfectly; and speaks them to men and Angels in the Church, according to the times and seasons of his divine administration and dispensation. Thereupon he is asked here; and gives charge and direction, ver. 16. See Rev. 1.1. and Zech. 1.9,11,12.

*{vision concerning the daily sacrifice}* That is to say, the things signified by it, and said of it, how long shall they continue?

*{transgression}* The idolatry of Jupiter Olympius, which Antiochus had set up in the Temple, instead of God's pure worship, 2 Mac. 6.1,2. which caused the extreme desolation of the people; in some, spiritually, by apostasy; in other some, bodily; by persecutions and torments, Dan. 11.31. and 12.11. 1 Mac. 1.57. and Chap. 6. vers. 7.

*{of desolation}* Or, *making desolate.*

**Verse 14.** *{he said unto me}* Christ the *Palmoni*, or wonderful Numberer, vers. 13. answered, not the *Angel,* that asked the question, as the Trem. Bible has it; but unto *Me;* for the comfort of his Church, whom it most concerned; and for whose sake, most likely, the Angel asked the question.

*{unto two thousand three hundred days}* Thus punctual is Daniel's prophecy. That is, until so many natural days be past, which make 6 years, 3 months and an half, or near upon 3 weeks; for so long, under Antiochus, was this desolation; from that defection of the people, procured by Menelaus, the High Priest, in his time, in the 142 year, 6 month, 6 day of the kingdom of the Greeks, or Seleucidae; as Josephus records in the 12 book, and Chap. 6. of his Antiquities. See 2 Macc. 4.23,25. when the host of God's people (concerning whom also the Angel inquired) began to be trodden under feet; and continuing, till the cleansing of the Sanctuary, building the altar, and sacrificing upon it, which was in the 148 year, 9 months, 25 day, 1 Mac. 4.52. This then is another reckoning, from another beginning differing from that, Chap. 7.25. and Chap. 12.7. That was of three years and ten days; beginning from the building of that idolatrous altar, 1 Mac. 1.54,59. and ending at the rebuilding of God's altar, and restoring his worship, 1 Mac. 4.52. This, of 6 years, 3 months, and 18 days, beginning and ending, as aforesaid. Or, if we should reckon these six years, 3 months, and about 19 days, from the beginning of Antiochus his first bloody persecution, in the beginning of the year of the Greeks, 143. to the year of his death, which was in the year, 149. the time will not much differ. See 1 Mac. 1.20. and 6.16.

*{days}* Heb. *evening morning.* So vers. 26. called, *νυχθήμερον,* 2 Cor. 11.25. The evening, being the beginning of the day of 24 hours; as the darkness was before light, Gen. 1. ver. 2,3. And so is the day reckoned, Gen. 1. ver. 8,13,19——23.31. and so Exod. 12.18. Lev. 23.32. The consideration whereof, and of the change of the beginning of the day, in our Savior Christ's time, may help to reconcile that difficulty, and difference of the day, wherein our Savior before his Passion, and wherein the Jews did eat the Passover, after his Passion.

*{cleansed}* It shall be purified from its uncleanness. Heb. *justified.* That is, set free by God's just judgment, from Antiochus his tyranny, who had made it a receptacle of idols: to be again according to its first institution, the holy Temple of the true God. This was done by Judas Maccabeus, 1 Mac. 4.52.

**Verse 15.** *{I, even I Daniel}* Vers. 1. Chap. 7.15,16. Here is his attention to the vision, and desire of knowing and understanding it more fully and distinctly.

*{stood before me}* According to the apprehension of his inward senses and sight.

*{as the appearance of a man}* Not Gabriel the Angel: he is commanded by this man, vers. 16. But Michael, that is, Christ; who, in this manner, declared himself to the old Fathers, as a preludium to his incarnation; how he would be God manifested in the flesh.

**Verse 16.** *{And I heard}* As in a vision; to my seeming and imagination, I heard such a sound, voice and words.

*{between the banks}* The range and banks, of the river itself, Chap. 12.7.

*{and said}* With a man's voice and words. How Angels communicate their notions one to another, is not a thing incident, or proper to this place, or inquirable.

*{Gabriel}* The name of an Angel; which signifies, strong man of God. See Chap. 9.21. Luk. 1.26. The only Angel in Scripture, which has a proper name: For Michael, Chap. 12.1. Jude 9. Rev. 12.7. is the name of Christ.

*{make this man}* This power to command the Angel, declares, that the Commander was Christ; who is true God, and the Lord, and head of Angels.

**Verse 17.** *{afraid and fell}* See Ezek. 1.28. and the *Annotations* upon it. Not to worship the Angel, but in a holy fear and reverence; being conscious of his own unworthiness, frailty and vileness. The presence and speech of an Angel, wrought thus upon holy Daniel, when the Angel drew near unto him.

*{Son of man}* Ezekiel and Daniel only, being in visions of Angels, are so spoken unto, Son of man: as Aben Ezra notes, Ezek. 2.1. See *Annotations* there.

*{Vision}* The words here are diversely rendered: and some understand them of a short time: but vers. 26. and the words themselves, here, speak of a long time, that the things foretold by that vision, shall come to pass at the appointed time, about the time of the coming of the Messiah, who shall fulfill the prophecies; and the old age; for to bring in a new one, by renewing the state of the Church. See Ezek. 38.8. Dan. 9.24.

**Verse 18.** *{Now as he}* Chap. 10.9.

*{deep sleep on my face}* That is to say, in my prophetical ecstasy, I lost all action and motion, as a man overcome with sleep, vers. 17. Rev. 1.17.

*{set me upright}* Heb. *made me stand upon my standing.* Did rear, refresh and strengthen me; as Ezek. 2.2. recover, and recomfort me.

**Verse 19.** *{and he said}* Continuing his speech, that was interrupted in the former verse.

*{what shall be in the last end}* The word, *Acharith* is a substantive. The meaning is this, what shall befall the Jews in the last end of this indignation, and wrath of God in this last affliction, which God shall send them for a punishment of their sins; meaning that great rage of Antiochus; and what he shall do then. After which shall be revealed the great and eternal reconciliation in Christ, Dan. 9.24. and 11.36. This last part of the vision he mentions, as the most regardable, and most desired by Daniel, to be understood, vers. 13.

*{the end shall be}* There shall be a certain prefixed time, for the lasting of this calamity. This verse strongly overthrows their error, which feign the Roman Monarchs to be meant by the legs of the image, Chap. 2. or by the fourth beast, Chap. 7. seeing that the Greeks are the dealers, in the end of wrath, spoken of Daniel's people. And it had been a strange thing, that this last vision, repeating the former, should leave out the Romans, if they had been spoken of before: seeing it were good to have that told; and God never omitted the good of his Church.

**Verse 20.** *{The Ram}* Ver. 3,4. Gabriel, orderly, proceeds from point to point, as the method and passage of the vision, directed him.

*{two horns}* Not meant personally, and punctually of Darius the Mede, and Cyrus the Persian, Chap. 6.28. For these two were not alive, when Alexander the great broke these two horns, here mentioned.

*{are the kings}* Are types of them, do signify them, the Kings of that kingdom, or empire. A typical, or sacramental phrase; and most plain to be understood; so vers. 21.

**Verse 21.** *{and the rough Goat}* So called for its roughness and horribleness. Of this, vers. 5,6,7,8. Here the whole Grecian empire is understood; Alexander, and his successors in the empire, divided.

*{Grecia}* This place proves, that of Javan, the Greeks come. Macedonia is herein comprehended.

*{the first king}* Namely, *Alexander* the great; the first of them that overthrew the Ram; that conquered the Medes and Persians. Of him personally is understood, vers. 5,6,7,8.

**Verse 22.** *{four kingdoms}* Ver. 8. Of many kingdoms arising from *Alexander,* four in the end bear the sway: and in time two; whereof, Chap. 11. will speak.

*{of the nation}* Namely, of the Greek, or Macedonian nation; though not of *Alexander's* posterity, Chap. 11.4. All the four were Grecians; and so continued the name of the Grecian empire, 1 Macc. 1.6.

*{in his power}* They shall not have like power, as had *Alexander.* So the particle, *in,* is used, John 3.21.

**Verse 23.** *{and in the latter time}* Viz. In the 137 year of the kingdom of the Greeks, or Seleucidae, 1 Macc. 1.10. Or, for then Antiochus Epiphanes, here spoken of, began to reign: though he entered not into Judea, till the year, 143. 1 Mac. 1.20. And after him, many of that race of the Seleucidae were Kings of Syria and Judea, for the space of 120 years, or thereabouts. But none of them are here spoken of; as not having a full power over the Jews: The yoke of their tyranny, being shaken off by the Maccabees, in this King's days, Chap. 7.22.

*{when the transgressors}* Among the people of the Jews. See 1 Macc. 1. vers. 11, &c. and vers. 43, &c. and Chap. 2.15. and 2 Mac. 4. This the cause of that fearful judgment and plague by Antiochus.

*{are come to the full}* Heb. *are accomplished.* Chap. 11.36. Gen. 15.16. Jer. 51.13. Mat. 23.32. 1 Thess. 2.16.

*{the full}* Of open impiety, and formal apostasy, Dan. 11.30. 1 Macc. 1.11,12. Gen. 15.16.

*{a King of fierce countenance}* Heb. *hard of face:* that is, brazen-faced, of whom, ver. 9, &c. Namely, Antiochus Epiphanes, chap. 7.20. and 11.21. who was impudent, as Deut. 28.50. and he is spoken of in the Maccabees, and heathen writers very much, for shameless impudence, against all divinity and humanity in his life and actions; of Polybius he was called Epimanes, that is, mad. Many Papists, would fain understand this King, and all that is here said of him, of their imagined Antichrist, or at least of Antiochus, as a type of him: But the more learned sort of them, desert that opinion and fancy.

*{dark sentences}* Heb. *Chiddoth:* and is properly hid things, and enigmatical; as Psal. 78.2. Hid mischiefs he minded, not hid parables: he was full of mischievous craft, and subtlety, his crafts master therein, vers. 25. by cunning and deep policy, to cover, and color, and bring about his own vile and cruel designs; and to discover the secret plots and underminings of others.

**Verse 24.** *{shall be mighty}* Vers. 9,10,11,12. and Chap. 11.

*{but not by his own power}* By his fraud and treachery rather, which he first used against his own father, brother, and nephew; by the perfidiousness and apostasy of the Jews, and their high Priests, and by the aid of foreign Kings. See Chap. 11.23. yet God's hand, and secret power, and providence, was not wanting in all this; as in the case of the Assyrian, Esay 10.15. and Babylonian, Jerem. 34.22. and 50.21.

*{wonderfully}* Prosper and prevail, against all lets and obstacles that would hinder him, from achieving his ambitious, tyrannical, mischievous practices, Chap. 11. All things should go on, and flow in, according to his own wish and will.

*{the mighty}* Great Kings and Princes, and especially them of Egypt, Chap. 11.25. against which he made two great expeditions.

*{the holy people}* Heb. *the people of the holy ones.* Namely, the Church of God; the Jewish people, both in his returns out of Egypt, and at other times.

**Verse 25.** *{Craft to prosper}* Chap. 11. vers. 21,23,24,32. his subtleties, and deep mischievous plots, shall not want his wished success.

*{magnify himself in his heart}* His prosperous success shall so elevate his spirits, and puff up his heart, 2 Mat. 9.7,8,10,12. and 5.21.

*{by peace}* Or, *by prosperity*; that is, under pretense of peace, and his specious promises of it; when men shall live secure in peace, and expectation of it, suspecting no other; then, and thereby shall he exercise cruel tyranny upon them. See 1 Macc. 1.30,31,32. 2 Mac. 5, &c.

*{prince of Princes}* Vers. 11. Apoc. 19.16. Against God himself, the Son, King of Kings, Chap. 11.36. invading his temple, making a prey and spoil of the sacred vessels and ornaments, and defiling that holy place with abominable idols, and pagan worship; and lifting up himself, and speaking against God himself; as if himself were God, 2 Macc. 9.8.

*{broken without hand}* Thus, Chap. 2.34,45. See Chap. 11.45. By sickness, stricken by the hand of God, and not by men; 1 Mac. 6.8,16. 2 Mac. 9.5,9,28. See Acts 12.22,23.

**Verse 26.** *{And the vision}* Concerning that determined number of days told, vers. 14. for the taking away of the daily sacrifice, and the treading under foot of the sanctuary, and host; which most troubled holy Daniel, and made him most inquisitive, after the time and durance of it; That is, here again avouched, and cleared.

*{is true}* In proper sense, needing no further explication: see the clearness and evidence of these prophecies, and the answerableness of the event: as if all this were not a prophecy, but an history; and so it is Chap. 11. which plainly argues the Spirit of God, in these holy Scriptures.

*{shut thou up the vision}* As Esay 8.16. Dan. 12.49. Keep it from the knowledge of the Chaldees and heathens; keep it to yourself in sacred silence, and reserve it in writing for posterity, without much publishing it for the present; because it is not so much for the use of this age, as for that which shall follow. See Esay 29.11. Rev. 5.1,2.

*{for many days}* Or, *after many days*. About three hundred years; are from the death of Belshazzar, unto the death of Antiochus Epiphanes. In whose days was greatest use to be made of this prophecy, and the event of things then would best clear, and expound it. See Rev. 22.10.

**Verse 27.** *{I Daniel fainted}* Chap. 7.28. and 10.8,16. Through fear and astonishment, at the greatness and sublimity of this divine vision; and at the evils that should befall God's house, and worship, and people.

*{rose up}* Out of that bed whereon he lay, seeing this vision, and sickening thereupon.

*{and did the King’s business}* Therefore he was in person at Babylon, and in vision only at Shushan. And continued still in that honorable office, and employment, Chap. 2.48. wherein thus appears his care and diligence.

*{I was astonished}* Still after the recovery of his sickness, and in the midst of his great state affairs.

*{none understood it}* As I had a command, to shut up the matter in the verse before; so I refrained my countenance, from open expressing of my grief; or, giving any intimation of the vision, or occasion of suspecting such a thing. The Trem. Bible reads thus; *Et non erat qui interpretaretur,* as if Daniel had wanted an interpreter of the vision, and were thereupon troubled and astonished.

## CHAPTER 9

**Verse 1.** *{In the first year}* Heb. *in one year:* the cardinal number, usually put for the ordinal. The same year Daniel was taken out of the Lions' den, and caused God's truth to be advanced generally. Now whether this was before his being in the den, or after, it is not so certain. This was in the year of the world, 3470. or thereabout: or, as others reckon, about 3430. In this Chapter, Daniel mindful of Jeremiah's prophecy, as is expressed, vers. 2. does now upon the expiration of that time, pray most zealously for the accomplishment of the deliverance then promised; whereupon he receives a speedy gracious answer, and message from God by Gabriel, of much more than he could think of, or hope for; even of the certain time, not only of the return of the people, and building of the city; but also of the coming of Messiah the Prince; and of his cutting off, and of the desolation of city, and Sanctuary, that should follow thereupon.

*{of Darius}* Heb. *Derjavesh:* Chap. 11.1. see Chap. 5.31. and 6.1. Darius seems to be a common name to many Kings of Persia. For so we read of Darius Hystaspis, Darius Nothus, Darius Ochus, Darius Codomannus, in heathen stories: as likewise Ahasuerus; or, as it is written in Hebrew, *Achashuerosh*, which signifies the *Great Head* of the people, and seems to be the same with Artaxerxes. And so we read of Artaxerxes Longimanus, Artaxerxes Mnemon or Memon. And haply, Xerxes is but a contraction of Artaxerxes; as Coniah is of Jechoniah: Thus usually one name, was common to many Kings of the same nation; thus was Merodac, among the Kings of Babylon: Pharaoh to the Kings of Egypt, in the most ancient times. And Ptolemy among the Lagidae. Thus Seleucus, Antiochus, and Demetrius among the Seleucidae. Arsaces among the Parthians. Tygranes among the Armenians. Aretas among the Arabians. Sylvius among the Latins; and Caesar among the Romans. Neither is it a thing unusual among Kings at this day. This Darius is here in the text sufficiently differenced from all others.

*{the son of Ahasuerus}* Or, *Cyaxares,* Chap. 6.1. See *Annotations* on Chap. 5.31.

*{of the seed of the Medes}* To distinguish him from Darius the Persian, Ezr. 4.24.

*{which was}* Or, *in which he was made, &c.*

*{The Realm of the Chaldeans}* For Cyrus, led with ambition, went about wars in other countries. See *Annotations* on Chap. 5.31.

**Verse 2.** *{In the first year}* This time is so diligently twice noted down; because it was the end of Jeremiah's seventy: and so the just occasion of this prayer of Daniel. And so we, when the time is come for the accomplishment of God's prophetical promises, should take hold of that occasion to serve God's providence, and to pray in faith for the fulfilling of them. Prayer must be in faith, Jam. 1.6,7. must rest upon God's promises; and be certain, Ezek. 36.37.

*{understood}* Attentively, accurately, considered, weighed, pondered in my mind, and knew.

*{by books}* For though he were an excellent Prophet, yet he daily increased in knowledge, by reading of the Scriptures, 1 Tim. 4.13. 2 Tim. 3.15,16,17. The Scriptures then were not lost and perished in the time of the Temple's overthrow, and the Babylonish captivity, as that apocryphal fable has it, 2 Esdr. 14.21——4.

*{to Jeremiah}* Jer. 25.11,12. and 27.5,6,7. and 29.10, &c. See 2 Chr. 36.21,22. Ezra 1.1.

*{seventy years}* Whereof fifty-two in utter ruin. Counting the beginning of the seventy not from the 11. of Zedekiah; but from the fourth of Jehojakim, and the first of *Nebuchadnezzar.* No doubt but the Jews carefully numbered these years, Zach. 1.12. another seventy years are mentioned: and those ending the second year of Darius, do derive their beginning from the 11. of Zedekiah.

*{the desolations of Jerusalem}* Daniel has the very Hebrew term of Jeremy, *Chorboth*; and by Jeremy he must be expounded, Jer. 25.11. *and this land shall become Chorboth*, (that is, *a wilderness*) *and an astonishment, and these nations shall serve the king of Babel seventy years*.

**Verse 3.** *{I set my face}* Of his body towards Jerusalem; as Chap. 6.10. of his soul to God; Eccles. 5.2. Psal. 25.1. and 62.8. This shows his earnestness, as Jer. 42.15. setting all other things aside.

*{To seek by prayer}* Ezek. 36.37. He uses here confession, deprecation, and petition. He speaks not of that ordinary prayer which he used in his house thrice a day; but of a rare and vehement prayer, upon the present extraordinary occasion; lest the sins of the people should cause God to delay the time of their deliverance, prophesied by Jeremiah.

*{with fasting}* Extraordinary helps, at extraordinary times, upon extraordinary occasions. Hereby sinners show their guilt and unworthiness; the forwardness of their minds to self-revenge, 2 Cor. 7.11. and 9.27. This makes prayer armed and winged. If Daniel needed these helps in prayer, much more we.

**Verse 4.** *{unto the Lord my God}* Not to false gods; not to Angels, Saints departed, images, relics, &c. but to the true God; and his God by Covenant; with faith and trust in him, to hear and grant his prayer. Thus must we pray to God, our God and Father, Mat. 6.9. Thus the wicked cannot pray.

*{my Confession}* Of mine, and my people's sins; and of God's justice and truth in punishing us. He begins with this confession, and persists in it, to the 16 verse.

*{O}* Or, *Oh.* Heb. *ana.* an interjection of exclamation, through the great grief of his heart for sin; and withal, of an humble begging and beseeching: to win grace and mercy.

*{Lord}* Exod. 32.31. Deut. 7.9,10,21. Neh. 1.5.

*{the great and dreadful God}* That is, Have all power in yourself, to execute your terrible judgments against obstinate sinners; as you are rich in mercy, to comfort them which obey your word, and love you. Thus affected, we should appear before him in prayer, with submission and fear.

*{keeping the covenant}* Faithful and true, Exod. 20.6. And therefore they might thank themselves and their sins, for their punishment in that Babylonish captivity. And yet now might hope and trust, in the keeping of his promise, by Jeremy, for their deliverance. Thus prayer must be carried on with faith.

*{to them that love him, and, &c.}* The love of God, and the keeping of his commandments evangelically, are never separated, Joh. 14.21.

**Verse 5.** *{we have sinned}* In his own name and the people's, as sustaining their person, he confesses their sins: and that without any extenuation; yea, with full mouth, and many aggravations. Here are four words, each gradually exceeding other. That they sinned, traded in it, and that obstinately, yea, maliciously and rebelliously, as if therein they would wage war against God. And all this, to profess and procure the deeper humiliation, the greater contrition and compunction. See 1 King. 8.47. Daniel seems to relate to that place, Psal. 106.6. A person or people, come not suddenly to be extremely evil, but by degrees.

**Verse 6.** *{Prophets which}* The duty of Prophets; and the height of sin, in not hearkening to them. The greater means of grace afforded, the greater is the sin, thereupon committed, Matth. 11.20.——25. Heb. 2.2,3. and 10.28,29. Kings and forefathers, are no rule of religion or life, Ezek. 20.18. Jer. 44.9,21. But all, from the highest to the lowest, are to be ruled by, and subjected to the word of God, Luk. 16.29,31.

**Verse 7.** *{righteousness}* He confesses God's justice in himself; and in his judgments for sin, Jer. 7.19. Psal. 119.137. He shows, that whensoever God punishes, he does it for just cause: And thus the godly, never grudge, repine, accuse him of rigor, as the wicked do; but acknowledge, that in themselves there is just cause, why he should so entreat them, Psalm. 51.4. Rom. 3.4. Psalm 119.75.

*{belongeth}* Or, *thou hast, &c.*

*{to the men of Judah}* Their prerogatives above other people, were no privileges for their sins, nor exemptions of them from punishment, but aggravations rather.

*{near and that are afar off}* In the neighboring nations, near to Judea, whether they fled, in Egypt, in Assyria, in the empire of Babylon.

**Verse 8.** *{To us}* Baruch. 1.15. Gen. 2.25. and 3.8. Psalm 119.80. Jer. 3.20.

*{to our Kings}* God is no accepter of persons; but wheresoever, and in whomsoever sin goes before, confusion follows after. And Daniel here, does not excuse the Kings, because of their authority; but rather charges them, as the chief occasions of these great plagues.

**Verse 9.** *{to the Lord}* You have a large subject, whereon to exercise your infinite mercy, which is to us, as it were, the Queen of your perfections; whereunto we now betake ourselves, and not to your justice, in this our height of iniquity: or, having sinned so excessively, as we confess we have, to you it belongs to use mercy and forgiveness; for you alone have power to do it, vers. 18. Thus he so mentions God's justice, for humiliation, as that he flies to God's mercies, to keep from desperation.

*{mercies and forgivenesses}* In the plural number, Lam. 3.22. mercies above all their sins; as Rom. 5.20. Jer. 31.29.

*{though}* No cause of God's mercy in us; but all solely and wholly in him, vers. 18.

**Verse 10.** *{The voice of the Lord}* He shows, that they rebel against him, which obey not his voice, in the mouths of his Prophets, but slight and condemn, so gracious a word of so good a God, so plainly set before them.

**Verse 11.** *{yea, all Israel}* Not some part only: an epidemical evil. The example of the multitude of outward professors, is no rule of life, Exod. 23.2.

*{therefore the curse is poured upon us}* As Deut. 27.15, &c. or, *the curse confirmed by an oath*, by an adjuration and execration. Yet they, out of a desperate impiety, wittingly and willfully contemned all.

*{Law of Moses}* Lev. 26.14, &c. Deut. 28.15, &c. and 29.20, &c. and 30.17,18. and 31.17, &c. and 32.19, &c. Jer. 42.18.

*{because we have sinned}* This plucked up the floodgate; broke the bank; opened the armories of God's wrath and vengeance.

**Verse 12.** *{confirmed his words}* Made good upon us, every one of them, by his judgments and punishments: so verifying his menaces, and showing his truth and power.

*{hath not been done}* This may be the abridgment of Jeremiah's Lamentations, Lam. 1.12. Amos 3.2. Luk. 12.47. Ezek. 5.9.

**Verse 13.** *{as it is written in the Law}* Lev. 26.14, &c. Deut. 28.15. Lam. 2.17.

*{yet made we not our prayer before the Lord}* Heb. *intreated we not the face of the Lord.* Prayed not in truth. For prayer, without turning away from iniquity, is no true prayer. The last and highest aggravation of their sin, is this; That the disease of it, mastered the medicine; grew the worse by the cure, and so became desperate: neither any threatenings, nor judgments, could humble them, or turn them to God, or make them truly pray unto him, Jer. 6.28,29. Esay 1.5.

*{understand thy truth}* Really perceive, and verily know the truth of the threats and promises; endeavoring thereby for true repentance according to your word; by means whereof we might have been partakers of the fruit of your holy promises.

**Verse 14.** *{watched upon the evil}* Of our sin, and his punishment. Providence has been watchful, and attentive to due vengeance; and to bring it in the prefixed time, and instant. See Jer. 1.12. and 31.28. and 44.27. 2 Pet. 2.3. Though for a time he might to some seem to wink and sleep; yet it is otherwise, as Psal. 78.65.

*{for the Lord}* He thinks he can never sufficiently justify God, and his judgments; nor enough confess and condemn their own sins.

**Verse 15.** *{And now}* After confession, now follows petition.

*{O Lord our God}* These titles show his power and willingness, whereby Daniel confirms his faith in prayer, Exod. 32.13. Psal. 33.12. and 144.15. and 135.4. and 74.20.

*{thou hast brought}* Lev. 26.45. and 22.33. Psal. 81.10. Esay 63.11, &c. Exod. 32.11. Neh. 1.10. and 9.9,10. Psal. 79.10,11,12.

*{gotten thee renown}* Exod. 14.18. and 32.12. Psal. 106.8. and 115.1. Neh. 9.10.

*{we have sinned}* One argument that God will hear our prayers, when we acknowledge our sins, and that there is no cause in us why he should hear our prayers, or do any good unto us; but that the whole cause is out of us, in him only, Psal. 25.11. and 51.3.

**Verse 16.** *{all thy Righteousness}* That is, according to all your merciful promises, and righteousness and truth in performing them; and according to your fatherly equity to your poor children, cruelly dealt with by the enemies, for causes unjust. See Psal. 31.1. and 22.5. and 51.14.71.2. Neh. 1.8,9. and 9.9. The performance of free and merciful promises, is righteousness, and justice, in God.

*{Thy fury}* No passion in God. But this and the like expressions are used in Scripture in a condescension to the weakness of our capacities, Micah 7.9. Rev. 15.7.

*{Thy City Jerusalem}* The place of worship, and type of the Christian Church, and that new Jerusalem which is above, Psalm 42.3,5. and 137.5,6. Esay 52.1.

*{for our sins}* He never conceals or extenuates their sins, but his finger is ever upon this sore; as the source of all their evils.

*{of our fathers}* Exod. 20.5. Not as those, Lam. 5.7. Ezek. 18.2. Jer. 31.29.

*{a reproach}* Lam. 1.8,9,11. and 2.15,16. and 3.14. Psal. 42.10. and 44.13,14,16. and 69.41,50,51. and 79.4. and 35.21. and 22.6. and 3.8. Mat. 27.39, &c. Psal. 74.10,18,22. and 123.3,4.

*{to all}* Psal. 69.20.

**Verse 17.** *{Now therefore}* Now it is time, Psal. 69.13. Esay 49.8. 2 Cor. 6.2. Now the seventy years of captivity are ended.

*{Cause thy face to shine}* Psalm 44.4. and 4.6. and 85.5,6,7. Thus after deprecation, follows supplication. Show by the effect your grace and favor, which like a vivifying and cheering sun, may dispel all these mists of extreme desolations.
