Though others say, that his father Evergetes, died of a disease, a natural death.

*{even to his fortress}* The foresaid Raphia, on the confines and frontiers of Egypt; and shall take it. Of which town, Strabo writes thus; beyond Gaza, stands Raphia, where the field was fought, between Ptolemy the fourth, and Antiochus the great. So famous God would have the Angel's remembrance, of fortress, to be.

**Verse 11.** *{And the King of the South}* Ptolomeus Philopator.

*{shall come forth}* In the fourth year of Antiochus the great.

*{with the King of the North}* Antiochus the great.

*{and he}* That is, *Antiochus* the great. Some read the words, as to understand this *he,* to be Ptolomeus Philopator; and yet vary not the sense.

*{but the multitude}* The great army of *Antiochus* the great. Polybius reckons the number of soldiers, on both sides; and also the manner of fight. It so fell out, at last, beyond all hope; that Philopator, with his sister Arsinoe, got so great a victory, and had such an hand over *Antiochus Magnus,* that if he had pursued with courage, he had not only recovered Syria, but might have spoiled him of his kingdom. *Antiochus* then escaped by flight, 3 Macc. 1.

*{his hand}* Into the hand of Ptolomeus Philopator.

**Verse 12.** *{And when he}* When Ptolomeus Philopator, has destroyed that numerous army of *Antiochus* the great.

*{his heart shall be lifted up}* As is, very usual, upon victories obtained.

*{and he shall cast down ten thousands}* These words may well be applied to the greatness of his victory; and his licentiousness growing thereupon; which *Justin.* Book 30. does note, to have been the beginning of the decay of his court. And as well applied to his pride against God, and his casting down ten thousands of the Jews, occasioned thereupon, which the third book of Maccabees, not among our apocryphal books; and Josephus in the 12 book, and 3. Chap. of his Antiquities, do set forth.

*{but he shall not be strengthened by it}* For *Antiochus* escaped his hands by flight; and after came again, and prevailed; as is declared in the verses following.

**Verse 13.** *{For the King of the North}* *Antiochus Magnus.*

*{shall return}* Having, for some time, by a league and peace between them, renewed his strength; and beginning to make less esteem of Philopator, by reason of his riotous and loose kind of living.

*{a multitude}* Of an army, greater than before.

*{and shall certainly come}* Heb. *in coming he shall come:* as certainly, so speedily and mightily.

*{after certain years}* Heb. *at the end of times of years.* That is, of the years of Philopator; he being now dead, by the means of Cleomenes, Polyb. 5. and his son Ptolomeus Epiphanes, being but a child of about four years old: *Antiochus* the great, should lay hold of that opportunity to invade, and assault him.

**Verse 14.** *{And in those times there shall many stand up, &c.}* Much sedition in Egypt; many of the young King's subjects rebelled, not enduring Agathocles, and such base governors, as his father Philopator, in his last and loose days, had set over them: many provinces, formerly subject to Egypt, then revolted: many Princes conspired and combined; Diodorus betrayed him; Antiochus Magnus joined to his aid, Philip, then King of Macedon; agreeing to divide betwixt them, the cities and provinces belonging to Egypt.

*{also the robbers}* Heb. *the children of robbers, factious persons, open contemners of God, and robbing him of his religion;* turning Egyptians, and gypsies (as we use the word) in religion, drawn thereto by Philopator in his latter days, 3 Macc.

*{of thy people}* The Jews. See *Annotations* on Chap. 9. ver. 24.

*{shall exalt themselves}* Shall not content themselves to be neutrals and spectators only; as formerly (*Josephus,* in his *Antiquities,* book 12. chap. 3.) But shall now be stirring and siding, and parts taking; and proudly, for the most part, join themselves to the *Egyptians,* against *Antiochus the Great.* See the third book of *Maccabees.*

*{to establish the vision}* Most expound thus; under show and pretext of religion, bragging to establish and fulfill that prophecy, Esay 19.18——25. which intends the spiritual union of Egypt with the Church in the days of the Gospel; upon this pretext many of them with *Onias* the Priest had fled into Egypt, had granted them, by Ptolemy, the country of *Heliopolis;* built themselves, a city, by the name of *Onias:* and a temple like to that in Jerusalem, wherein they sacrificed, and which remained till the time of *Vespasian* the Roman Emperor. These refractory apostates now exalted themselves in pride for Ptolemy against *Antiochus.* Thus Hierom, out of *Josephus,* in his *Antiquities,* book 13. cap. 6. and in his War of the Jews, book 7. c. 30. Others thus; that this vision and prophecy, being fulfilled in, and upon these, should serve to this use, to the Church, and people of God, to establish and confirm them the more, in believing the truth of the rest of the vision and prophecy.

*{but they shall fall}* By the revenging hand of *Antiochus,* in his recovering of Syria, and Judea; and in this his expedition against Egypt, prevailing against *Ptolemy Epiphanes,* and punishing them as rebels against him; as in the next verse.

**Verse 15.** *{So the King of the North}* *Antiochus Magnus, Antiochus the Great.*

*{shall come}* Against *Ptolemy Epiphanes;* the eighth horn.

*{and cast up a mount}* Raise up *trenches* and *mounts,* for the besieging and taking of *castles, forts,* and *cities,* in Judea, and in Egypt.

*{the most fenced cities}* Heb. *cities of munitions.*

*{and the arms of the south}* The *armies, captains,* such as *Scopas, Europus, Menocles,* and *Damenxenus;* not the whole strength and strongholds of *Ptolemy* in Judea, Syria, and Egypt.

*{shall not withstand}* Shall not be able to stand it out, and resist *Antiochus Magnus.* See Ezek. 31.18. The history of the accomplishment of all this, is set down by *Josephus* in his *Antiquities,* book 12. c. 3. and by Hierom likewise.

*{neither his chosen people}* Heb. *the people of his choices:* his most choice and select commanders and soldiers: as the phrase is used, of the choicest sepulchers, Gen. 23.6.

**Verse 16.** *{But he that cometh against him}* *Antiochus the Great.*

*{and he shall stand in the glorious land}* Heb. *Tzebi,* the land of ornament, the goodly land, that is in Judea. See cap. 8.9. and the *Annotations* there.

*{which by his hand shall be consumed}* Ruined, say some. Others thus; which by his hand shall be consummated; that is, his victories there perfected, according to his own desire; and that by the ready help of the Jews, deserting Ptolemy, and assisting him. And this better agrees with the histories of those times; as appears in *Josephus* his *Antiquities,* book 12. cap. 3. and by *Antiochus Magnus* his own epistle there recorded; and by Polybius in his 11 book.

**Verse 17.** *{He shall also set his face, &c.}* *Antiochus Magnus* shall thus enterprise against *Ptolemy Epiphanes,* and Egypt again, with all his force and fraud.

*{and upright ones with him}* Heb. *or much uprightness, or, equal conditions with him;* that is, say some, he shall be followed in his enterprise by many Jews, called upright ones, according to the sense of Deut. 32.15. Or, as others, with the greatest show of uprightness and integrity, with the fairest and most equal terms and conditions; yet minding hereby to circumvent *Ptolemy Epiphanes.* That color and specious pretext was the matrimony presently mentioned.

*{thus shall he do}* Attempt some hostility. But that not altogether succeeding according to his wish, he shall proceed to the mention of peace, by the motion of marriage.

*{and he shall give him the daughter of women}* The faire *Cleopatra,* his beautiful daughter; and so make *Ptolemy Epiphanes* his son-in-law. A like practice was that ver. 6. but with a more upright intent there.

*{corrupting her}* Commanding and instructing, and inveigling her by some great guile to kill her husband; that so he, under color of her right, and for her, might come to have the government of the kingdom of Egypt.

*{but she shall not, &c.}* Not stand to the wicked purpose and promise made by her to her father, to make away her husband, nor be for him; but shall stand on her husband's side, and be for him. See Chap. 2.43. Livy book 37.

**Verse 18.** *{after this he shall turn his face to the Isles}* Missing his purpose in Egypt; yet eager still to enlarge his dominion and renown, he shall make great preparation by sea and land, and set upon divers countries and provinces far off, and beyond the seas, both *islands,* and *continents;* as *Cyprus, Phocaea, Samos, Rhodes, Colophon, Euboea, &c.* and some cities of Greece, subject to, or confederate with the Romans. So in Gen. 10. the countries of Grecia, all about Hellespont, are termed. And this appears to be so in Appian, Livy, and Plutarch, Scipio.

*{and shall take many}* As appears to be truly verified in the heathen histories before cited.

*{But a Prince}* Not any particular King; but a legate, officer, or consul of the Roman commonwealth, *Marcus Acilius Consul, Lucius Pretor,* and *Lucius Scipio,* the two famous brethren.

*{for his own behalf}* Heb. *for him;* that is, for himself, or to himself, in the behalf, and to the behoof of the Roman commonwealth.

*{shall cause the reproach offered by him to cease}* Heb. *his reproach;* that is, take off, and wipe away that reproach offered by *Antiochus Magnus,* to the Roman commonwealth in that his naval expedition, and gaining of so many places and cities in the Midland sea, in lesser Asia and Grecia. The Romans shall recover them from him.

*{without his own reproach}* With honor, revenging the wrong done by *Antiochus Magnus.*

*{he shall cause it to turn upon him}* He shall not only force *Antiochus Magnus* to retire himself from all those places which he had taken from the Romans, and their confederates, and friends to the disgrace of the commonwealth of Rome; but shall also overcome him in a pitched battle, though *Hannibal* was with him, and spoil him of part of his dominions, and force him to accept of a disgraceful and reproachful peace.

**Verse 19.** *{Then he shall turn his face toward the fort of his own land}* He shall be forced to return into Syria, and shamefully to fly and shelter himself beyond *Mount Taurus,* and to keep himself in strongholds, without daring to wage war anymore.

*{but he shall stumble, and fall, and not be found}* He shall fly to Apamea, Susa, and the utmost bounding cities of his kingdom: and at last was killed in a tumult by the Elymeans, seeking to defend the Temple of Bel, which he went about sacrilegiously to rob and spoil. This was the catastrophe of that great *Antiochus,* the father of *Antiochus Epiphanes.* All these things are recorded to be thus verified, according to the Angel's predictions here, by the many human histories of those times.

**Verse 20.** *{Then shall stand up in his estate}* Shall succeed him in his kingdom. So ver. 7. and 21.

*{a raiser of taxes}* Heb. *one that causeth an exactor to passe over:* an exactor of tributes and taxations, relating likely to Heliodorus, 2 Macc. 3. sent by this man. And this is Seleucus Philopator, sometimes called Soter, the elder son of *Antiochus Magnus,* called here *a raiser of taxes,* by reason of his infamous covetousness, and cruel taxations laid upon his kingdom. And he is the ninth horn.

*{but within few days}* Heb. *unities of dayes.*

*{he shall be destroyed}* That is very soon after that he sent Heliodorus against the Jews, at the instigation of Simon; as appears, 2 Macc. 4.

*{neither in anger}* Heb. *angers:* not killed in battle, nor by open violence of war; but poisoned by the treachery of Heliodorus, in favor to Antiochus Epiphanes. See *Annotations* on Chap. 7.8,20,24.

**Verse 21.** *{a vile person}* *Antiochus* the younger brother of *Seleucus Philopator,* and son of *Antiochus Magnus.* This man is surnamed *Epiphanes,* the illustrious, *per antiphrasin;* opposite to that title the Angel here gives him, which is according to the truth of his conditions, and well suiting also with that other title, which others of the heathen writers gave him, *Epimanes,* that is, mad, or furious. So Polybius in Athenaeus, by whom his manners, qualities, and deeds, are noted to be strange and execrable. This is the 10 horn, in whom the iron of the Syrian leg does end; that little horn, and plucker away of three horns. See Chap. 7.8,24. and Chap. 8.9. On this man, his expeditions against Egypt, and his rage against the Jews, the rest of Daniel goes. For the better learning whereof, we shall premise these general notions; agreeing best in our judgments with Daniel's text, and with the truth of history answerable thereunto. We conceive that *Antiochus Epiphanes* beginning his reign in the 137 year of the kingdom of the Greeks, 1 Macc. 1.10. and after his gaining of the kingdom of Syria, vers. 22,23,24. of this Chapter, made four expeditions into, and against Egypt: and three several times executed his furious rage, in spoiling and bloody persecuting the Jews. His first expedition into Egypt, seems to be that intimated, 2 Mac. 4.21. compared with Chap. 5.1,11. and that in the beginning of his reign, after he had settled himself in the kingdom of Syria. His second expedition is set down, vers. 25,26,27. of this Chapter. And in his return, he executed his first furious rage against the Jews, vers. 28. and this was in the 143 year of kingdom of the Greeks, and 6 of his reign. See 1 Mac. 1.16——29. and 2 Mac. 5.1.——22. His third expedition is set down, vers. 29,30. of this Chapter, and in his return he executes his second and most outrageous fury against the Jews, in his own person, taking away the daily sacrifice, and setting up the abomination of desolation, vers. 31——39. and this was in the year, 145. and 8 of his reign: and then Mattathias, and his sons the Maccabees, began to rise and maintain wars, and obtained wondrous victories against his armies, captains, and commanders. See 1 Mac. 1.54. and Chap. 2.1,70. and Chap. 3.1——27. His fourth expedition into Egypt is set down, vers. 40,41,42,43. of this Chapter; and after his return he executes his third fit of fury against the Jews, vers. 45. himself going into Persia, and commanding his deputy *Lysias* to root out the Jews; and this was in the year 147. and 10. of his reign. See 1 Mac. 3.27——60. and also, Chap. 4. and 1.5. In the year, 148. and 11 of his reign, *Judas Maccabeus* rebuilds God's altar, and restores his true religion, 1 Mac. 4.52. and in the beginning of the year, 149. and 12 of his reign, *Antiochus Epiphanes* dies miserably, 1 Macc. 6.16. though the author of the second book of *Maccabees* put his death in the year 148. 2 Mac. 11.21,38.

*{to whom they shall not give the honour of the Kingdom}* The states of Syria shall not give it him, after the death of his brother. His father *Antiochus Magnus* had left as a pledge in Rome, for assurance of his faith, and performance of conditions, 1 Mac. 1.10. After his father's death, his brother *Seleucus* being King, had sent his son *Demetrius* to Rome to be an hostage and pledge there; in the stead of this *Antiochus Epiphanes.* Upon his return by the way, he procures his brother *Seleucus* to be poisoned, as was said; by *Heliodorus,* one of *Seleucus* his friends and favorites: and now coming to Syria, he deals, as follows.

*{but he shall come in peaceably}* He shall neither be created, nor crowned King; neither get the kingdom by force of arms, but come in by fraud, ver. 24. and chap. 8.24,25.

*{and obtain the Kingdom by flatteries}* Pretending to govern the kingdom for *Demetrius* the true heir, his brother's son, now an hostage at Rome; and he only, as tutor to his nephew: and after, he shall win men's hearts with presents, flatteries, and secret practices, and so abusing his nephew; and deceiving the peers and people, he shall install himself in the kingdom; principally by the means of *Eumenes,* and *Attalus,* neighboring Princes; as *Appian* relates in his Syrian history.

**Verse 22.** *{And with the arms of a flood}* And with strong and sudden armies, as of a great flood of waters.

*{shall they be overflown from before him, & shall be broken}* Those that would not give him the honor of the kingdom. Others understand this, but not so well, of *Antiochus Epiphanes* his first expedition against Egypt, that the Egyptians should thus be overflown and broken by him: Those arms and armies of Egypt, that formerly like an inundation had overflown Syria, should now be overflown and broken, overrun and overcome by him.

*{yea also the Prince of the covenant}* Shall be broken by him. Who this was, is diversely interpreted. We understand Seleucus Philopator, the brother of Antiochus, who was the author and head of a covenant between them two, for the invading of Egypt. And to that end had sent for him from Rome: and him Antiochus Epiphanes procured to be poisoned, while himself was on the way in his journey from Rome, before he entered Syria, and got the power of it into his hands. Yet others, applying it to his first expedition into Egypt, do understand it thus; that Antiochus Epiphanes having in this his first expedition overrun Egypt, and in battle by Peleusium overcome Eulaeus, and Lenaeus, two principal peers of Egypt, who managed the affairs of Egypt in the young years of the King Ptolomeus Philometor, the son of Ptolomeus Epiphanes, was thereafter drawn to a covenant with Ptol. Philometor, to become guardian as it were to Philometor, being his sister Cleopatra's son. And of this covenant making, one Tryphon a prime peer in Egypt was principal and Prince: and that afterwards Antiochus soon caused this Tryphon the Prince of this covenant to be made away, that he might with more freedom rule as he list, and do what he would. And yet there are some others, who by *Prince of the Covenant,* understand it of God, chap. 8.11.25. who is the Prince of his people, and joined to them by his holy covenant: and so in this verse they will have summarily comprehended Antiochus his two greatest enterprises; namely upon Egypt, and upon Judea: both which are afterwards more particularly specified.

**Verse 23.** *{And after the league made with him}* The forementioned league with his brother Seleucus Philopator, as we have said. The other understand it of the league made with his nephew, his sister Cleopatra's son, Ptolomee Philometor, King of Egypt.

*{he shall work deceitfully}* As his manner was, ver. 24. and chap. 8.24,25. and as after is declared, 23,24.

*{for he shall come up}* Into Syria, as meant here. Others, into Egypt, to *Memphis* the royal city, in the heart of Egypt; not as an enemy, but as a friend, and confederate, and a tutor or guardian to the young King.

*{and shall become strong with a small people}* Bringing with him few followers, a small train, because he will not give the Egyptians cause of suspicion, but stout men, though few; and devoted to his service; whom he placed in divers forts, and thereby made himself master of the principal holds of Egypt. But we rather apply this to his manner of gaining upon Syria; not by strength of arms; but by flatteries, and fair pretenses.

**Verse 24.** *{He shall enter peaceably, even upon the fattest places of the Province}* Or, *into the peaceable or fat, &c.* He shall enter into Egypt, which at that time did enjoy peace and plenty of all things; or, rather upon Syria.

*{and he shall do that which his fathers have not done}* He shall place garrisons in the strongholds; taking possession of the greatest part of Egypt, which his forefathers could never do. We still do rather understand this of his manner of gaining the kingdom of Syria from the right owner Demetrius, the son of his elder brother Seleucus.

*{he shall scatter among them, &c.}* His infinite bountifulness and prodigality to his garrisons, and soldiers, 1 Mac. 3.30. thereby to keep them the more firm unto him.

*{yea and he shall forecast his devices}* Heb. *think his thoughts:* cunningly to get and gain the rest of all the strengths of Egypt, or Syria rather.

*{even for a time}* All this was but for a time; even till Ptolomeus Philometor grew up in years; who then raised his strength, and freed himself and the Egyptians of all his garrison; thus they, who still understand all this of Antiochus his first expedition into Egypt. But we, with other learned interpreters, still applying this, as all the rest in this and the two former verses, to his gaining of Syria from his nephew Demetrius, conceive, that for a time he spent himself in this employment. The compassing of this business took up the first year of his reign.

**Verse 25.** *{And he shall stir up his power}* This is the second expedition of Antiochus Epiphanes against Egypt, as was said on the 21 verse, which was about the fifth year of his reign, and 142 of the kingdom of the Greeks, 1 Mac. 1.20. and undertaken upon pretense to aid and right Physcon against his brother Ptol. Philometor King of Egypt.

*{King of the South}* *Ptolome Philometor.*

*{but he shall not stand}* *Ptolome Philometor* shall not stand in battle, but be overcome by *Antiochus Epiphanes.* Some, as we said understand this of that former battle by Peleusium, whereof we spoke upon the last words of the 22 verse.

*{for they shall forecast devices against him}* Many of the states and potentates of Egypt shall betray their King, and being corrupted and bribed by Antiochus Epiphanes shall treacherously forecast, and plot and devise secretly with Antiochus against him; as is further showed in the next words.

**Verse 26.** *{yea they that feed, &c.}* His intimate familiars, favorites, and counselors shall be his overthrow. Haply mistrusting the age and experience of their young King; and fearing the craft and cruelty of Antiochus, and the danger of their own estates if Antiochus prevail, they underhand comply with him, to the destruction of their own King.

*{and his army}* The army of *Antiochus Epiphanes.*

*{shall overflow}* Overrun and overcome the very great and mighty army of Ptolome Philometor.

*{and many shall fall——}* Of the Egyptian army.

**Verse 27.** *{And both these Kings}* Of the North and South; Antiochus Epiphanes, and Ptol. Philometor.

*{hearts}* Heb. *their hearts.*

*{shall be to do mischief}* When they are treating of a peace, they shall intend mischief one to another.

*{at one table}* When they shall feast one another, and interchange mutual courtesies; yet all these shows shall be but feigned, both of them retaining their former enmities.

*{but it shall not prosper}* This false dissimulation and counterfeit peace will not hold.

*{for yet the end shall be at the time appointed}* Vers. 29. The end of that peace shall be at the time appointed by God, who has all times, and all occasions and opportunities in his hand, at his disposal. See ver. 40.

**Verse 28.** *{Then shall he return}* *Antiochus Epiphanes,* into Syria, after that pretended peace in the 143 of the kingdom of the Greeks, 1 Mac. 1.20. and fifth or sixth year of his reign.

*{with great riches}* The spoils of Egypt.

*{and his heart shall be against the holy Covenant}* Some understand this of that covenant and peace which he made with Ptol. Philometor. But that was a false and fraudulent covenant, as has been said. This holy covenant seems rather to relate to that which God was pleased to make with his people the Jews; as ver. 30. See Exod. 24.7,8. Deut. 5.2. and Chap. 29.1. The heart of Antiochus was against them, and their holy religion; with his dealings against Egypt; his doings against Judea still fall out. It he takes still in his way, 2 Macc. 5.1,11. 1 Macc. 1.16——24. This was his first outrage against the Jews, as was said in the *Annotations* on vers. 21.

*{and he shall do exploits}* Outrageous and bloody actions against the Jews; destroying them, sacrilegiously profaning and robbing the Temple, changing their laws and religion, and every way most furiously tyrannizing over them, 1 Mac. 1.17, &c. The spoils of the Temple and city, added to his former spoils of Egypt, so increased his riches, that in his return to Antioch, his mind was puffed up exceedingly, 2 Macc. 5.11——22. 1 Macc. 1.20——24.

**Verse 29.** *{at the time appointed}* By God, ver. 27. about two years after; the 8 of his reign, and 145 of the kingdom of the Greeks; when Ptol. Philometor, was now reconciled with his brother Physcon; and had entreated the aid and auxiliary forces of the Romans. This was his third expedition.

*{south}* Egypt.

*{but it shall not be as the former, or as the latter}* This expedition shall not prove such, so successful to him; He shall not obtain the victory, nor subdue Egypt; as in the former expedition he did, vers. 25. or in the latter he should, ver. 40. Others better understand both of the two expeditions before this.

**Verse 30.** *{For the ships of Chittim, &c.}* The fleet of the Romans, which lay in the harbors and havens of Italy, Cilicia, and Graecia, that they might the more commodiously command all the parts of the Midland sea. See Gen. 10.4. where *Kittim* is the third son of Javan, from whom the Grecians came: whence is that, Num. 24.24.

*{shall come against him}* At the suit and request of the King of Egypt, being now besieged with his mother Cleopatra, in the city of Alexandria by *Antiochus Epiphanes.* Many histories do record how the Senate of Rome sent legates or ambassadors to *Antiochus Epiphanes* to command him to raise his siege, and abstain from war against their confederate King. He answered, he would demur and deliberate thereupon. Whereupon *Popilius Laenas,* one of the legates, drew a circle about *Antiochus Epiphanes,* and required his peremptory answer before he should go out of that circle. And *Antiochus Epiphanes* withdrew his siege and the war. Thus *Florus, Paterculus,* and others.

*{therefore he shall be grieved, and return}* Grievously vexed to be thus imperiously dealt withal, thus overmastered, and overborne; and forced to leave his conquest of Egypt.

*{and have indignation against the holy Covenant}* Vers. 28. He leaving Egypt, and taking Judea in his way, as his use was, he shall turn his wrath, and pour it out, and spend it against the holy covenant. This was his second outrage against the Jews. See *Annotations* on vers. 21.

*{so shall he do}* Do it to purpose; in a most furious and outrageous manner, as is set down in the nine verses following.

*{and have intelligence with them, &c.}* Formerly with Jason, whom he had made high Priest in the first year of his reign, 2 Macc. 4.7——21. 1 Macc. 1.11——16. Now with Menelaus, whom he had made high Priest, removing Jason, 2 Macc. 4.23. and 5.5,6. and with other apostates; by their advice to infest the Jews.

**Verse 31.** *{and armies shall stand on his part}* That great army and power which he brought now with him out of Egypt; and a great faction of wicked Jews now joining with him. See 1 Mac. 1.43.

*{and they shall pollute the sanctuary}* Entering into it: touching, and taking away the holy things, instruments, and vessels of it, sacrilegiously robbing and spoiling it, 1 Mac. 1.21——25. 2 Mac. 5.15. See 2 Mac. 3. and Chap. 6.4.

*{of strength}* Or, *the strength.* Jerusalem; a place strong by situation. Or the Temple, which was the rock of the great King, Zech. 9.12. and 11.2. they shall pollute it by slaughters of men in it, 2 Mac. 5.12,13,14.

*{and shall take away the daily sacrifice}* Chap. 8.11. See the annotations there, and Chap. 12.11. See the like, Chap. 9.27.

*{and they}* The commanders and soldiers of *Antiochus Epiphanes.*

*{shall place the abomination that maketh desolate}* Or, *astonisheth;* Chap. 12.11. This is either the garrisons of idolatrous and abominable soldiers, that brought desolation upon the Temple and city. See 1 Mac. 5.22,25. and Chap. 6.4. and 1 Macc. 1.33——41. See the like in the times of the Romans, after our Savior's Passion, Dan. 9.27. Mat. 24.15. Or else, the image of Jupiter Olympus, 1 Mac. 1.54. 2 Mac. 6.2. Or, an idol's altar, set upon the altar of God, 1 Mac. 1.59,60. and Chap. 6. vers. 7. Because of which things, the godly Jews refusing to consent to idolatry, the Temple, and God's service were made desolate. See *Annotations* on vers. 11.

**Verse 32.** *{And such as do wickedly}* Apostate Jews, that did prostrate themselves to the idolatry of the heathens.

*{corrupt by flatteries}* Or, *cause to dissemble.* By gifts, promises, and allurements, he shall foster and confirm them in their apostasy and idolatry; by their examples to draw on others.

*{but the people that do know their God}* That profess and worship him, according to his will and word.

*{shall be strong}* The wicked shall apprehend them as evildoers. Such is the sense in some translations. But rather thus, *shall be strong;* that is, they shall show themselves constant, and persevere in his pure service.

*{and do exploits}* Manfully resisting, and overcoming all temptations, enduring all torments, and cruel kinds of death. Read the histories of old Eleazar, 2 Mac. 6. and of the mother, and her seven sons, 2 Mac. 7. And then rose Mattathias, and his sons the Maccabees, who did those strange exploits.

**Verse 33.** *{and they that understand, &c.}* They whom God shall lively illuminate by his Word and Spirit, shall instruct and strengthen their brethren, by their doctrine and example. Some others, understand this of a certain religious society, newly set up in those days, called Assideans, that is to say, pious, holy, understanding, and very zealous in the law of God. See 1 Mac. 2.42. and 7.13. 2 Mac. 14.6.

*{yet they shall fall by, &c.}* Antiochus Epiphanes, by his bloody agents, shall persecute, oppress, and slay them, both the instructors, and instructed, 1 Macc. 1.40, &c. and 1.2,3,4. Chap. and 2 Macc. 5,6,7,8. Chapters.

*{many days}* Many is not in the original: neither see we much warrant for it. Days, may imply a short time: and the history seems best to agree with that.

**Verse 34.** *{They shall be holpen with a little help}* Few helpers shall they have: Mattathias, and other Asmoneans, called Maccabees, shall help them, 1 Mac. 2.1,39. and 3,4,5. Chapters, 2 Mac. 8.1.

*{but many shall cleave to them with flatteries}* Among the Jews many, who have run themselves before into idolatry; when they shall see some enlargement by the means of the Maccabees, shall join themselves to their brethren; but dissemblingly, without any inward faith and piety; and to small advantage of their brethren.

**V. 35.** *{And some of them of understanding}* That did instruct others, vers. 33.

*{shall fall}* By the sword, &c. as vers. 33. in this bloody persecution and execution, by Antiochus Epiphanes, and his instruments.

*{to try them}* Or, *by them.* This is the end, why God suffers them thus to fall by their wicked enemies; as Chap. 12.10. namely, to try their faith and obedience, as gold is tried in the fire.

*{and to purge}* away their corruptions; as dross from the silver in the furnace; and chaff from the wheat, by the fan.

*{and to make them white}* as the cloth with fullers' soap. See Mal. 3.2,3. Thus God suffers it for their good, as well as for his own glory.

*{even to the time of the end; because, &c.}* The time determined, by the counsel of God, to put an end to these persecutions, vers. 27. which shall not last still, but shall end in a short time; as is after more explained. See Mat. 24.6.

**V. 36.** *{And the King shall do according to his own will}* Antiochus Epiphanes; as the article, here prefixed, does manifestly denote, that king spoken of before. He shall prosper in his prosecutions and purposes; things shall succeed a while, according to his wish and will. Which success, shall so puff him up with pride and arrogancy, as after follows. In this, and the three verses following, Antiochus, and his doings, may well serve our turn, as a type of that grand Antichrist and his doings, set forth by the Apostle Paul, 2 Thess. 2. and by Saint John in the Apocalypse. For that Antichrist directly imitates this proud, idolatrous, and bloody tyrant. But all that hereafter follows in the rest of the angel's prophecy, as well as that which is past, does literally, and exactly agree to Antiochus Epiphanes, and to him only, as shall after appear; and was fulfilled in him; and is limited to the time of this Antiochus, Chap. 12.7,11,12,13. And therefore to imagine, that the angel here, should abruptly break off from Antiochus Epiphanes, and take off his prophetical speech from him and the Jews, that were Daniel's people and care; and fall into a prophecy of Antichrist, and the Church under the Gospel, towards the end of the world, is a thing so inconsistent with the whole context, as forces the chief interpreters, among the Papists themselves, to forego this fancy of Antichrist, which otherwise they would willingly embrace for their own advantage, and to agree with us, in continuing the understanding and application of the text, to Antiochus Epiphanes. And so all Bellarmine's arguments, fetched out of this context of Daniel, to prove the Pope not to be Antichrist, are vain and frivolous. And much less are they to be heard, that would apply this, and that which follows, to Pompey the Great, or to Vespasian and Titus; or to Constantine the Great; or to the Turk and Ottoman family.

*{above every God}* And so in the latter part of the 37 vers. Not only assuming divine honors, but ascribing to himself even more than to any god, 2 Mac. 9.8,10,12. spoiling their temples; in Elemais, or Persepolis; and in Egypt, changing their rites and religion; and prescribing what service and worship, and to whom he pleased, 1 Mac. 1.41,42. & 3.29. *Josephus,* in his *Antiquities,* Book 2. Chap. 6,7.

*{marvellous things against the God of gods}* The true God of Israel, Esay 45.14,18,21. Exod. 18.11. *Speak great words against the most High*, Chap. 7.25. and *stand up against the Prince of Princes*, Chap. 8.25. forbidding his religion; circumcision; Sabbaths; sacrifices; defiling his temple; burning the books of the law; *changing times and laws*; polluting all God's worship; instituting a new religion; setting up chapels of idols, idol-altars, and abomination of desolation upon the altar. See all this accomplished, 1 Macc. 1.43–62. *Josephus* in his Antiq. Book 10. Chap. 14. of Wars, Book 1. Chap. 1. against Appion, Book 2.

*{till the indignation be accomplished}* Till God's wrath and just indignation be satisfied, in the accomplishing of those things, which he, by his decree, has determined to be done, in the visitation of his people; to destroy those open apostates, and gross hypocrites; and to try, purge, and whiten his own. Neither shall the indignation remain unaccomplished, upon Antiochus himself, when he has filled up his measure; to the full heightening of God's wrath against him,

*{shall be done}* Heb. *is done, or hath been done.* The time past is used for the future; to note out the certainty of the event.

How suitable to all, set down in this verse, Antichrist's doings should be, is set down, 2 Thes. 2.4. and how answerable thereunto, the doings of the Popes of Rome have been, is more than manifest.

**V. 37.** *{the gods of his fathers}* He shall be impious, and without any fear of God at all. The gods of his fathers and ancestors, which men naturally are addicted to follow, even those he shall disregard and despise, disdain to worship them; and by letters and edicts, disannul his own ancient religion; and bring in the idols of the Grecians, which were among the Syrians. See 1 Mac. 1.41, &c. 2 Mac. 2.5. & Chap. 9.2. And though his predecessors, his brother and father, honored much the God of Israel, and temple of Jerusalem, 2 Mac. 3.2,3. *Josephus* in his *Antiq.* book 12. chap. 3. Yet he, in the highest nature, dishonored both; as was showed before.

*{nor the desire of women}* Women are strong motives: and the desires and loves of women, very strong, 2 Sam. 1.26. And Antiochus had many women and wives; and among them, one a Jewess, and of much esteem with him: yet none of them, nor of their desires could prevail with him, either to desist from his impious and idolatrous designs in religion; or to suffer them to enjoy the use of their own. Some suspect his inclination to the sin against nature, to be here meant. Others, that he was a man without all natural affection, regarding neither men nor women, kith, nor kin, any ways crossing his own vile ends.

*{nor regarding any God; for, &c.}* See Annotations on vers. 36.

**V. 38.** *{But in his estate}* Heb. *As for the Almighty God; in his seat he shall honor; yea, he shall honor a God whom, &c.* Thus rather, and the meaning is instead of the true God, he shall honor the God of munitions, whom he shall set up in his strongholds, a God whom his fathers knew not.

*{the god of forces}* Or, *Munitions.* Heb. *Mauzzim.* This signifies, strongholds. So ver. 19. and 31. Judg. 6.26. Is not here a proper word; either for the true God, or for an idol; but a common word; as in the next verse. Jerusalem and the temple, both of them were great strengths; and the God of them, the most strong God of all, the Almighty God; though he be not here meant by *Mauzzim.* What Papists, and some others, fancy of the god *Maozim,* is frivolous.

*{in his seat}* In his place and stead; rather than in his estate. Yea, in God's own city and temple. See 2 Mac. 6.2. This idol he guarded with munitions, 1 Mac. 1.35,38.

*{shall he honour, I say honour}* This doubling the word, seems to show the angel's holy passion, at the indignity of this fact of Antiochus.

*{a God whom his fathers knew not}* See vers. 37. and therefore called a strange god, vers. 39. This was Jupiter Olympius; of whom, see 2 Macc. 9.2. And this was an idol of Athens, which his fathers knew not. The Syrians, and his fathers, worshipped Apollo, Diana, Atargatis, as Strabo writes in his sixteenth book of Geography. Of the ancient gods of Syria, a general mention is made, Judg. 10.6. 2 Chron. 28.23. See Lucian of the Syrian goddesses.

*{with gold, &c.}* With gifts and offerings of such things, according to the manner of idolaters.

**V. 39.** *{Thus shall he do, &c.}* He shall seize upon Jerusalem; and especially upon the strong city and fort of David, whereby it being well fortified, and for a garrison, and furnished with men and munition, he shall put down God's true service, and establish his own abominable idolatry in the temple, 1 Mac. 1.35,38. as if this idol were his protector, against the service of God. He shall make strong places of defense, where he shall set up his strange god. Others, translate, and expound thus, And *he shall commit the munitions of strengths,* or, *of the most strong* God, *to a strange God.* That is, Antiochus shall commit the city Jerusalem, that strong and fortified place, and the strong temple in it, to *Jupiter Olympius,* as its tutelar god. But the words bear not this so well.

*{whom he shall acknowledge and increase with glory}* Those whom he shall discern to acknowledge this idol, to favor his side, to comply with him in his wicked counsels and enterprises, though base wicked fellows, those apostate priests, and Gentilizing Jews, and obscure strangers; those he will advance to honors and places, to dignities and preferments; as Jason before, 2 Mac. 4.7,8. and *Menelaus* after, 2 Macc. 4.23,24,50. whom, for money, he made high priests, and governors of the people, 1 Maccab. 3.35,36.

*{and he shall cause them to rule}* Antiochus shall make those base and unworthy fellows, rulers over the people of the Jews; offices, and places of government he shall prefer them into.

*{over many}* The article, *it,* prefixed, seems to denote the many, those pious and religious worthy ones among the Jews; as chap. 9.27.

*{and shall divide the land for gain}* Both honors and lands, for price he shall set to sale, and distribute among his factious followers, Jews, or foreigners, 1 Mac. 3.36.

**V. 40.** *{And at the time of the end}* Determined by God. See ver. 35. about two years after his setting up of that idolatry, vers. 31,38. and two years more before his death.

*{that the King of the South}* *Ptolomeus Philometor.*

*{push at him}* Fight with him; as chap. 8.6,7.

*{and the King of the North}* *Antiochus Epiphanes.*

*{shall come against him like a whirlwind, &c.}* With great force and celerity: with armies by land and sea. See the like, Esay 17.3. This was his fourth and last expedition against Egypt. See Annotations on ver. 21.

*{and shall overflow}* Expel him out of his kingdom, under the color of aiding and righting Physcon the younger brother of Ptolomæus Phylometor; to whose side, Antiochus Epiphanes, for his own ends, was still addicted. Florus, book 46. Zonoras tom. Annal. 2. Justin.

**V. 41.** *{the glorious land}* Judea, ver. 45. See Chap. 8.9. and 11.16. And this is the third and last fury executed upon the Jews; as is further showed and dilated, ver. 45.

*{overthrown}* By the incursions of Antiochus Epiphanes, and the spoils that he shall make in this war, at his return out of Egypt.

*{shall escape, &c.}* He spared these, because they took his part, held on his side, and fought against the Jews and Maccabees, 1 Macc. 5. Josephus in his Antiquities, book 12. Chap. 11.

**V. 42.** *{upon the countries}* All about; the neighboring nations, none shall escape, save those, ver. 41.

*{Egypt}* against which he made so many expeditions.

**V. 43.** *{over the treasures, &c.}* As he was profuse in giving, ver. 24. So as greedy in getting, ver. 39. See 1 Macc. 3.28–32.

*{Libyans, and the Ethiopians}* A people of Africa, borderous to the Egyptians, subjects, or auxiliaries, to Ptolomy Philometor, they shall now upon the victories of Antiochus, follow his steps and service, and receive his pay. Lybia sometimes taken for the larger country of Africa, sometimes for that part next Egypt, called Cyrenica, Act. 2.10. So here the remoter parts of Lybia are called Phut, Nah. 3.9. of Phut Gen. 10.6. Ethiopia or Cush is sometimes taken for Arabia in Asia, whereof Moses' wife was, Num. 12.1. & Zerah the king, 2 Chr. 14.9. But most commonly for that beyond Egypt; and so here.

**V. 44.** *{But tidings out of the East}* Phraates the king of the Parthians, invading the east borders of the kingdom of Antiochus; as Zonaras makes mention. Some add Araxas king of Armenia.

*{and out of the North}* The navies of the Romans, lying in the havens of Cilicia and Cyprus: also the stirs and troubles against him in Judea, lying north from him, being now in Egypt.

*{shall trouble him}* 1 Macc. 3.27,31.

*{therefore he shall go forth}* Out of Egypt.

*{with great fury}* 1 Macc. 3.27,35. Full of indignation. See his like fury another time, 2 Macc. 9.4,7.

*{to destroy, &c.}* The Parthians and Persians, and the Jews especially, 1 Macc. 3.27,35,36,37.

**V. 45.** *{And he shall plant}* Antiochus himself, after his return out of Persia, say some, Josephus in his first book of the War of the Jews, Chap. 1, Or, Lysias, and other commanders under him, by authority from Antiochus, 1 Macc. 3.32, &c. See Annotations on ver. 21. & 41.

*{palace}* Appoint to settle his own court and residence there, the better to subdue and keep under the Jews. Antiochus his army did entrench itself, having the royal tent set up, for a sign of full power given to his captains, in Emmaus, near to Jerusalem, 1 Macc. 3.40. and 4.3.

*{between the seas}* The dead sea, and the midland sea, in the heart of Judea.

*{In the glorious holy mountain}* Heb. *mountain of delight of holiness.* Meaning Hierusalem. See ver. 41. Psal. 48.1,2. 1 Macc. 3.40. Lysias pitched in Emmaus hard by Jerusalem.

*{come to his end}* The end and utmost bound of his dominions, say some; that is, to Elymais, or Persepolis, a famous city of the Persians; where going about to rob the temple, and spoil the city, he was by the inhabitants forced to flee with dishonor and shame, there being none to help him, 1 Macc. 6.1–5. 2 Macc. 9.1,2. But though this story be true; yet his end here rather denotes the end of his life, which seems to be in Babylon, 1 Macc. 6.4,8,9,13,16. in the 148 or 149 year of the kingdom of the Greeks: compare 1 Macc. 6.16. with 2 Macc. 11.21,23. and his end was miserable enough, without any help, as the next words show; and the story relates, 2 Macc. 9.10,12. Thus was the horn broken without hand, Chap. 8.25.

## CHAPTER 12

*{And at that time}* Namely, in the year 147 year of the kingdom of the Greeks, when Antiochus begun his expedition into Persia; and in his absence committed to Lysias the government of his young son Antiochus Eupator, and of all his affairs, from the river Euphrates, unto the borders of Egypt, with charge to destroy the Jews, to root out the remnant of Jerusalem, and take away the memorial of them from that place, 1 Macc. 3.31–38.

*{shall Michael}* At that needful time shall Christ, who is *Michael,* *in the form of God*, &c. Phil. 2.6. See Chap. 10.13. and the Annotations there.

*{the great Prince, &c.}* See Ezek. 34.24. Apoc. 1.5. and 12.17. and 15.3. *the King of the Saints*, and Chap. 17.14.16. and Chap. 19.16. Eph. 1.21.

*{which standeth, &c.}* Who is their captain, Josh. 5.14,15. their protector against all their enemies, Gen. 48.16. Psal. 54.7. stands up in their defense.

*{thy people}* The angel's continued speech to Daniel. See Chap. 9.24. and the Annotations there. The Jews then, Daniel's people must necessarily be meant here, and their trouble; and so what follows is all meant of them, and not of the Church of Christ among the Gentiles under the Gospel, towards the end of the world.

*{a time of trouble, such, &c.}* Never the like cruel and general persecution for religion and conscience, since there was a nation, through the whole state and story of the nation of the Jews, to and till that same time of Antiochus Epiphanes. And this one speech also might have kept up the right understanding of Daniel, and kept us close to those times, and people of the Jews, and withheld us from drawing men beyond Christ, for these troublous times and stories.

*{thy people shall be delivered}* By Judas Maccabeus, and others. The pronouncing of the Jews' deliverance out of these troubles, still makes the foresaid cause the plainer; That these words, and so all the rest of the angel's speech, as plainly appears, ver. 7,10,11,12. are bounded within the limits of the time and story of *Antiochus Epiphanes.* And withal we see, that his people's extremity in troubles, Christ takes as his best opportunity and season, for his working their deliverance. And this temporal deliverance of God's people mentioned here, through his much mercy is carried on in after ages, and promoted by several degrees, till it receive its full and final accomplishment in and by Christ's second coming.

*{every one that shall be found written in the book}* Of God's decree for this deliverance aforesaid. This metaphor of writing in a book, we first meet withal in *Moses,* Exod. 32.32 where it is called God's book, and of his writing. Afterwards in David, Psa. 69.28. In the New Testament, Paul calls it the *Book of life,* Phil. 4.3. And so our Savior, Apoc. 3.5. and Chap. 22.19. and the angel, Apoc. 17.8. and St. John, Apoc. 20.12.15, who also calls it the *Book of Life of the Lamb,* Apoc. 13.7. *the Lamb's book of life*, Apoc. 21.27 See Ezek. 13.9. These said places and passages have relation to God's eternal decree of election, and to those persons that are so elected. And these here mentioned in this text may seem to be some of that number. Deliverance they shall have, either temporal, or spiritual.

**V. 2.** *{And many of them that sleep, &c.}* The meaning of the whole verse seems to be this, That notwithstanding that deliverance formerly promised, yet it shall come to pass, that many of the elect shall then be slain in that persecution of Antiochus, and *sleep in the dust of the earth*; their enemies meanwhile living and triumphing: and that therefore that deliverance is not to be taken as commensurate, and bounded only within the narrow limits of this transitory life, but to be extended to the consideration of eternity: And so though many of the elect should be slain in that persecution; and so in mercy taken out of the miseries of this life: yet the time shall come, even the time of the general resurrection of all men, when these shall awake out of the sleep of death, to everlasting life; and their persecuting enemies, notwithstanding their flourishing and triumphing estate in the short time of this life, shall then awake *to shame and everlasting contempt*. See Heb. 11.35. 2 Macc. 14.46.

*{many}* All the dead shall arise again, *many,* sometimes signifies *all;* as Rom. 5.19. Here many does distribute them all into two sorts, two multitudes, two ranks, as our Savior does, Mat. 25.33, &c. The Hebrew accent set upon the word *רבים* *many,* so singles it out, as shows it is equally to be referred to both members of the distribution following, Joh. 5.28,29.

*{that sleep, &c.}* A description of death, usual in Scripture. The soul lives in the sleep of death; as it does in the sleep of the body in this life.

*{shall awake}* Out of that sleep; shall rise, and live again, Job 14.12. Esay 26.19.

*{some to shame}* See Esay 66.24. Rom. 9.21.

**V. 3.** *{And they shall be wise}* Or, *teachers,* pointing specially at those, Chap. 11.33,35. such as were in Matthias, Judas Maccabees, Eleazar the old man, and valiant martyr, and others: and generally the true believers who in this life are lightened by the Holy Spirit, shall enjoy the light of glory in the kingdom of heaven.

*{shall shine, &c.}* See Mat. 13.43. and Chap. 17.2. Judg. 5.31. The glory of heaven is here laid out in shining terms; a taste whereof Daniel felt in the glory of the angel Gabriel, that was before him now; and in that glorious vision, Chap. 10. See 1 Cor. 15.41.

*{that turn away, &c.}* Heb. *and they that justify the many;* that is, that by their teaching the truth, bring the excellent ones, or elect of God, to true faith, which only justifies and sanctifies in newness of life. See 1 Tim. 4.16. James 5.19,20. And yet these words point still more specially to those, Chap. 11.33,35. There are degrees of glory in heaven.

*{many}* The article affixed shows some reference to those *many,* Chap. 9.27. and Chap. 11.39. where this article is prefixed likewise.

**V. 4.** *{But thou, O Daniel, shut up, &c.}* The use and clear understanding of this prophecy is, not for this present time, or age, ver. 9. but for that time which is ordained by God, for the fulfilling and accomplishing thereof. See Chap. 8.26. and the Annotations there. In that prophecy, Elam, and Madai, and Javan were named. Here they are so plainly described, that any learned heathen would tell rightly what kingdoms must be meant; See also the Annotations on chap. 8.1. These secrets then of heaven which are concredited to Daniel's trust in this divine prophecy, he must write them in a book: he must keep them as a most precious treasure. Shut and seal them up for the use, and benefit, and comfort of God's people in those after ages, and needful times, 300 years yet to come, to the time of Antiochus Epiphanes, and hide them from others, for whose sakes they were not revealed, and who would make no good use of them, but abuse them rather. See Mat. 7.6. See Apoc. 10.4. John is bid not to seal up his prophecy, Apoc. 22.10. because some part thereof was presently to take place.

*{even to the time of the end}* The time appointed of God for the fulfilling of those prophecies; when God shall prove by the event, that he has not foreshowed these things in vain.

*{many shall run to and, &c.}* That is, though there be few now that are careful, as you are, of the future state of the Church, and therefore will little mind these prophecies for the present, and are unworthy to whom they should be revealed; yet the time will come, when many will run after this knowledge, and seek to and fro up and down for it, and will increase and abound therein. So that Daniel should not grieve for the present shutting and sealing up of such divine and profitable prophecies; for when the matters shall break out, even in the time of *Antiochus Epiphanes,* yet then the Church should remain in the midst of that bloody persecution, and horrible devastation: and many then should run, read, search with all diligence after these things; as appears they did in the books of the Maccabees. And in the event and issue, they will gain by God's Spirit, full knowledge of these things which are obscurely foretold. See Esay 29.18. Jer. 23.20.

**V. 5.** *{there stood other two}* Other two angels; besides Gabriel, Chap. 8.16. & 9.21. & 10.10. who hitherto has revealed all these prophecies to Daniel, being sent to that end: and besides him also that was clothed in linen, who sent Gabriel, Chap. 10.5,11. that is, Christ our Savior. So as, the greatness of these matters is here set forth by the attendance of the angels, who *desire to look into them;* as 1 Pet. 1.12. and also the certainty of them, by the number of witnesses that do testify them, Deut. 17.6. and Chap. 19.15.

*{the one on this side, &c.}* On all sides, from all parts, the angels are ready to do service to the Church, and to learn of Christ, what may make for the good and comfort of it.

*{bank}* Heb. *lip.*

*{of the river}* *Tigris* or *Hiddekel.* See Annotations on chap. 10.4. and on Chap. 8.16.

**V. 6.** *{And one said}* One of the two. Whether of them; or whether both, either together, or the one after the other, is not certain. No doubt, they both had the same mind and desire, were both one to this point.

*{to the man clothed in linen}* To Christ. He is that *Palmoni,* the numberer and revealer of secrets. See Chap. 10.5. and Chap. 8.13. and the Annotations on both places. See Rev. 10.1.

*{which was upon, &c.}* Or, *from above.* See Chap. 8.16.

*{How long, &c.}* See 1 Pet. 1.12. Exod. 25.20. Ephes. 3.10. So Chap. 8.13. See Apoc. 6.10. The angels understanding of these future miseries of the Church, in a sympathy and fellow-feeling of them, do desire to know the end and termination of them. Much more should we take thus to heart the miseries, and the state of the Church, Psal. 137. and 122. and 87.7.

*{these wonders!}* Of these admirable predictions, and strange accidents which shall befall the Church. The angels admire them; though they cannot comprehend the reasons of them.

**V. 7.** *{And I heard}* Daniel heard both the question and the answer. Both were made chiefly for Daniel's sake, and the Church's behoof.

*{the man}* Christ, 1 Tim. 2.5. Rom. 5.15. 1 Cor. 15.21,47. as a forerunner of his incarnation in the fullness of time.

*{clothed in linen}* The attire here shows his priestly office. See Chap. 10.5. and the Annotations there. He is a sacrificer holy, and higher than the heavens.

*{the waters of the river}* Psal. 93.4. And this answers to Job's speech, that God *treadeth upon the high waves of the Sea,* Job. 9.8. See Dan. 8.16. and 12.6.

*{held up his right hand, &c.}* Rev. 10.2. an angel stands with his right foot upon the sea, and his left foot on the earth: and swears, lifting up his hand to heaven, vers. 5,6. and the right hand is commonly used in the gesture and ceremony of an oath. See Gen. 14.22. Deut. 32.40. Here both hands are lifted up, held towards heaven; it may be, because of the two angels, one on each side of him.

*{swore by him that liveth for ever}* Apoc. 10.6. By him alone we must swear, the God of truth, that knows our hearts, and all things, and can revenge a lie, Zeph. 1.5. Heb. 6.16. See Matth. 5.34,35,36. and Chap. 23.16–23 and Deut. 32.40.

*{for a time, times, and an half}* Or, *part:* after those three years, and 10 days, Chap. 7.25. See the Annotations, there. There was just that time from the building of that abominable altar, and abolishing of God's worship, and changing it into the heathenish worship of idols, till and to the restoring of God's true worship and service by *Judas Maccabeus,* 1 Mac. 1.54. with Chap. 4.52. See Rev. 12.14. See Annotations Chap. 8.14. and 7.25.

*{and when he, &c.}* When God shall scatter the power of *Antiochus,* that infests the holy people, the Jews. Others thus, When *Antiochus Epiphanes* shall have brought the Jews to extremity, then God shall miraculously relieve them, Chap. 11.34.

*{all these things shall be finished}* The grievous calamity and persecution ended; and the deliverance of the Church afforded.

**V. 8.** *{I understood not}* Daniel ingenuously confesses, that he heard the answer; but understood not the secret of these times, distinguished in this manner; what they were, and when they should begin. He heard it in general terms; but understood it not, so particularly, and plainly, so distinctly, and fully, as he fain would, and desired to do. And indeed, these matters were to be spoken in such sort, as that until the age of their execution, the particulars should not be more evident. The trial of the faithful required that; who else for that time would have withdrawn themselves.

*{O, my Lord,}* He inquires of Christ, as the angel did and not of them.

*{what shall be the end of these things?}* His ignorance makes him renew and repeat the question. Such was his holy desire after these things. So 1 Pet. 1.10,11. He a little varies the question, yet in words, rather than in substance, asking not when and how long, but, *what shall be the end of these things?* what kind of end those wondrous troubles, and this deliverance should have?

**V. 9.** *{And he said}* Christ said; *the man clothed in linen,* ver. 6,7.

*{Go thy way, Daniel}* Surcease, forbear, be contented with this revelation, with that answer, it is enough; inquire no further, demand no more concerning these things, the further knowledge whereof is reserved to its proper time, and belongs neither to you, nor to the age wherein you live, ver. 13. See Act. 1.6,7. Knowledge, and the revelation of it, has its several steps, measures, and gradations. And we must not be overcurious, to be wise above what is written; but wise to sobriety, 1 Cor. 4.6. Rom. 12.3.

*{for the words}* That is, the things. *Words* in the Hebrew, is usually taken for *things:* and the Hebrew expression here is, *these words.*

*{are closed up and sealed}* Barred up, hid, involved in some obscurities and perplexities; not so plainly and perfectly to be understood, as you do wish, ver. 4.

*{till the time of the end}* Till that time be come which is prefixed by God, for the ending, fulfilling, and accomplishing of these things. The event is the clearest interpreter of prophecies. Then are they fully, and clearly, certainly, and perfectly known.

**V. 10.** *{Many shall be purified, &c.}* Chap. 11.35. See the Annotations there. This is a recapitulation of that prophecy concerning the calamities of that persecution by Antiochus Epiphanes. And here is the good end and use of it to God's children, thrice told.

*{but the wicked shall do wickedly}* The wicked Jews, like reprobate silver, shall not be bettered in the furnace of those afflictions; but the worse by them, as Jer. 6.28,29,30. They shall yield to those fiery trials and temptations, and be overcome by them. Yea, and many out of the height of their wickedness shall willingly comply with Antiochus Epiphanes in all his wicked and idolatrous abominations. See Chap. 11.32,34. 1 Mac. 1.11–16. and Ver. 43, and 52. 2 Macc. 4.9–16. See Rev. 22.11.

*{and none of the wicked shall understand}* The profane apostates will not understand God's work in those times of persecution, nor attend to these prophecies, nor draw any benefit of instruction or consolation from them. See Psal. 28.5. & 92.5,6,7. They shall understand nothing because they are blinded.

*{but the wise shall understand}* Those, ver. 3. and Chap. 11.33. They shall mark, observe, and understand well God's work in those times, and these prophecies and predictions fulfilled in them; neither shall they be drawn away with the error of the wicked. See Hos. 14.9. Psal. 107.43.

**V. 11.** *{And from the time}* In the close here, Christ does favorably condescend to some further designation of the time, so much insisted upon; and to parcel out, and particularize the same more than formerly he did. So that Daniel's desire and inquiry is in part satisfied, and not altogether repulsed; as that repulse, Act. 1.7.

*{that the daily sacrifice shall be taken away}* Whereof, see Chap. 8.11,12,13,14. and Chap. 11.31. See also that, Chap. 9.27.

*{and the abomination that maketh desolate set up}* Heb. *to set up the abomination that maketh desolate,* or *astonisheth,* Chap. 11.31. and 8.13. See the annotations there. This time was the 145 year, 9 month, 15 day of the kingdom of the Greeks, 1 Mac. 1.54. & 4.52. That abomination, Chap. 9.27. is another.

*{there shall be 1290 days}* That is, three years, six months, and fifteen days, from the time formerly mentioned in this verse; which extend to six months, and five days, after the rebuilding of God's altar, and restoring of his true worship and service in, Vers. 7. At this period of time, likely, some notable mercy was afforded them, not mentioned in Scripture. But the books of Maccabees do declare many singular victories afforded the Jews after the rebuilding of God's altar; over all their neighboring enemies. See 1 Mac. 5. chapter throughout: and as some relate, a singular victory over Antiochus himself: as also Antiochus his kind of repentance for his wrongs done to the Jews; together with a vow of his own conversion to Judaism; and of granting them the free exercise of God's true worship, and of honoring them, the temple, and their religion, sundry ways: as we may read at large, 2 Mac. 9.13–28. See 2 Mac. 11. and it seems at the end of this time, Judas Maccabeus had prosperous success against the Ammonites, with their captain Timotheus.

**V. 12.** *{Blessed is he}* Much happier shall his condition be.

*{that cometh to the 1335 days}* That is, 45 days after the former. For then Antiochus Epiphanes died, 1 Mac. 6.16. 2 Mac. 9.28. though the very month and day of his death be not expressed in that history. And so the Jews were fully freed from the fear of him.

His last vows and promises, if they had been known to them, yet they could not have confided in the words of such a man. But his death made all sure. Nor after did his race, and successors get dominion over them. Thus we have four terms set concerning the persecution of Antiochus, 1. 2300 days, Chap. 8.14. which makes 6 years, 3 months, and 20 days. This ends before the sanctuary was defiled. 2. a time, two times, and a part of times, Chap. 7.25. & 12.7. that is, three years, and ten days, during the which, the daily sacrifice was discontinued. 3. Here is one time of 1290 days, which ended at such time as religion was fully restored after the purgation of the temple. 4. 1335. which ended at the death of Antiochus.

**V. 13.** *{But go thou thy way}* Go on in the way and course of your life that yet remains, content with this my revelation: look for no more visions, but rest in this. That which God had yet further to reveal for the comfort of his church, he would reserve for other times. As Ezra, Zachary, Haggie, Malachy, were raised up afterwards. God would adorn his temple at the reedifying thereof with some prophetical visions.

*{till the end be}* The end of your life: prepare for it, to end it comfortably. It cannot be far off, you are now so old.

*{and thou shalt rest}* In peace; free from these calamities, and from the miseries of this life, Apoc. 14.13.

*{and stand in thy lot at the end of the days}* That is, stand still in that lot of prosperity and honor, mentioned, Chap. 6.28. till, and at the end of your days. Others thus; you shall rest, in your grave; and stand up, at the general resurrection of the dead, in your lot, of celestial inheritances, and heavenly glory; prepared and allotted to you and other prophets, at the end of the world, for the days of eternity. See Psal. 1.5. 2 Tim. 4.8. Every one then has his lot; not all the same measure of glory.
